6.3 Site Plan 
The WWTP will be located within the Wendover Pines subdivision near the northeast corner of 
the development (see Figures 3, 4, and 5) and treated effluent is planned to be discharged to a 
nearby wash that flows towards the Sitgreaves National Forest.  The WWTP will be provided 
with noise, odor, and aesthetic controls.  Odor generating proc esses will require a setback of a 
minimum of 100 feet from the nearest property line in accordance with ADEQ requirements.  
 
To improve the economics of the system the WWTP shall provide f low equalization. The 
WWTP process will include nutrient removal, filtration and disi nfection. The WWTP will be 
enclosed within a masonry block wall. The site will be provided  with power, lighting, 
communications, and security provisions. The WWTP will be opera ted by an operator with 
appropriate certification required by ADEQ. 
 
6.4 Regulatory Requirements 
The construction of the Wendover Pines WWTP will require the is suance of several permits and 
require several reviews and approvals from a number of Federal,  State, and local regulatory 
agencies. Likely permits and approvals to be considered include: 
 
208 Plan Amendment.  An amendment to the Area-wide Water Quality Management Plan in 
accordance with Section 208 of the Clean Water Act may be requi red prior to obtaining any 
reviews or approvals of any other facets of the project.  The N orthern Arizona Council of 
Governments (NACOG) administers this program and will determine  if a 208 Amendment is 
required. 
 
Discharge Permit.  A National Pollutant Discharge Elimination System (NPDES) permi t is 
required by USEPA and is administered by ADEQ. This permit will be required for the discharge 
of effluent to a dry wash. The washes within the Wendover Pines  development including the 
wash that the WWTP will discharge into, have been determined by  t h e  U S  A r m y  C o r p s  o f  
Engineers to be non-jurisdictional under Section 404 of the Clean Water Act.  
 
Discharge Standards Variance Request.  In addition to applying for an NPDES permit, a 
Discharge Water Quality Variance Request may be required for le vels of nitrogen and

<!-- page 225 -->
Wendover Pines  Coe & Van Loo Consultants, Inc.  
Preliminary Wastewater Master Plan  Project No.: 1.14.0334301  
 
12 
N:\14 Coe and Van Loo II LLC\0334301\Enviro\Reports\Wendover.Pines.WWMP.docx              November 6, 2019 
phosphorus. The waiver request will need to be submitted to ADE Q to be checked for 
completeness prior to forwarding the application to USEPA for t he final determination. The 
determination will be based on the cost of conventional technol ogies versus the cost of 
technologies required to achieve the required surface water qua lity standards in accordance with 
ADEQ and USEPA requirements.  Additionally, there can be no obj ection from affected parties, 
including Native American Communities and others, downstream of the point of discharge. 
 
Aquifer Protection Permit.  This permit will be administered by ADEQ and is required to 
ensure that the WWTP facilities protect groundwater and meet th e requirements of the Best 
Available Demonstrated Control Technology (BADCT). The key requ irement is to meet the 
water quality standards in accordance with the Arizona Administ rative Code. This permit will 
serve as authorization to proceed with construction. 
 
Reuse Agreements.  If reuse of the treated effluent is to be considered in the fut u r e ,  a  r e u s e  
agreement for each reuse site will need to be in place before r eclaimed water can be delivered.  
These agreements will need to be submitted to ADEQ for review and approval. 
 
Well Drilling Permit.  Permits may be required at the discretion of ADEQ for the groun dwater 
monitoring wells for the points of compliance at the WWTP. Thes e permits will need to be 
obtained from the Arizona Department of Water Resources (ADWR). 
 
Sludge Disposal Permit.  Disposal of sludge from the WWTP will need to be compliant with  
US Code of Federal Regulations, 40 CFR Part 503.  Class A sludg e will be required for land 
application for public use to en sure the required pathogen redu ction.  Class B sludge may be 
disposed of at a permitted landfill or designated land applicat ion sites.  The permitting process 
will be administered by ADEQ for USEPA.  Additionally, compost may come under the further 
jurisdiction of the United States  Department of Agriculture, de pending on the fertilization 
content of the sludge. 
 
Air Quality Permit.  In order to operate a diesel f ueled engine backup electric powe r generator 
at the WWTP, an air quality permit will be required.  This program is administered by ADEQ. 
 
US Forest Service Permit.  In order to discharge effluent onto National Forest Land, and t o 
establish an odor setback easement, a US Forest Service Permit will be required. 
 
Stormwater Pollution Prevention Plan.  In order to construct, a Stormwater Pollution 
Prevention Plan (SWPPP) will need  to be prepared prior to initi ating grading activities.  This 
permit is required to ensure that appropriate measures are take n to prevent erosion and 
sedimentation of waterways.

7.0 WASTEWATER NETWORK MODELING 
7.1 Network Analysis Domestic Demands 
Modeling for Wendover Pines sanitary sewer network was accomplished by using spreadsheets 
to calculate accumulative flows and compare elevations and velocities. The spreadsheet uses 
criteria from Section 3.0 and site information to ensure the network is meeting the required 
design criteria.  
 
Criteria used in the sewer capacity analysis were developed per ADEQ’s Engineering Bulletin 
No.11 (July, 1978). Results from the sewer capacity analysis meet the criteria presented in 
Section 3.0. Wendover Pines sewer capacity analysis includes on-site sanitary sewer to the 
ultimate outfall location at the northeast corner of the development, See Figures 3 and 5 for the 
location of the wastewater treatment plant (WWTF).  
 
See Appendix A for Wendover Pines gravity sewer capacity analysis. See Appendix B for 
Wendover Pines low pressure grinder pump analysis.

8.0 SUMMARY 
This preliminary wastewater master plan presents the proposed sanitary sewer network, and an 
overview of the proposed facilities necessary to provide sewer treatment to the proposed 
Wendover Pines development. The following summarizes CVL’s findings of the proposed 
sanitary sewer system to serve Wendover Pines. 
 
o Wendover Pines is an approximate 294.5 acre proposed residentia l development including 4 
acres of commercial development. There are approximately 450 pr oposed residential units 
planned, ranging in lot size from cabins to 2 acre lots.  
o There are no existing sanitary sewer infrastructures or facilities within the property. 
o The Wendover Pines development is estimated to be partially served by a low pressure sewer 
system outflowing to a gravity sewer system which is utilized b y the remainder of the 
development. 
o Daily flows for Wendover Pines are anticipated to be: 
 Average Daily Flow:                               122,875 gpd 
 Peak Daily Flow:                    285,938 gpd  
o Out of the approximate total 450 proposed units; 113 units are estimated to be served by 
Gravity and 337 units are estimated to be served through the lo w pressure system. Each unit 
served through the low pressure  system will require an individu al grinder pump. As 
Wendover Pines is developed and final lotting established, a determination will be made as to 
whether each lot will be on gravity service or require an indiv idual grinder pump connected 
to the low pressure system. 
o The proposed gravity sewer system will consist of 8-inch gravit y sewer lines with 48-inch 
diameter manholes at a depth of less than 12-feet. 
o The proposed low pressure sewer system will consist of an estim ated 28,779 LF of low 
pressure force mains ranging from 2.5- to 6-inches. 
o The commercial areas are likely to be served by gravity. 
o The proposed sanitary sewer network for Wendover Pines satisfie s the design criteria listed 
in Section 3.0. 
o A wastewater treatment system will be necessary for Wendover Pines as currently envisioned 
and will be sized to treat the proposed average daily flow of t he peak month and peak flow. 
Design for the wastewater treatment plant will be determined on ce a detailed lotting layout is 
established for the development.

<!-- page 239 -->
10
and up to 24 average, single-family homes with
consent of the factory.
Considerations include:
• Wetwell and discharge piping must be protected from freezing
• Model and basin size must be appropriate for incoming flows, including peak flows
• Appropriate alarm device must be used
• Suitable location
Daily flows above those recommended may exceed the tank’s peak flow holding capacity and/or
shorten the interval between pump overhauls. The company should be consulted if higher inflows
are expected.
The final selection will have to be determined by the engineer on the basis of actual measurements
or best estimates of the expected sewage flow.
Grinder Pump Placement
The most economical location for installation of the grinder pump station is in the basement of the
building it will serve. However, due consideration must be given when choosing an indoor location. If
there is a risk of damage to items located in the basement level, other provisions should be made
during basement installation or an outdoor unit should be considered.
Considerations such as ownership of the pumps by a municipality or private organization and/or the
need for outdoor accessibility frequently dictate outdoor, in-ground installations. For outdoor installa-
tions, all GP models are available with high density polyethylene (HDPE) integral accessways
ranging in height up to 10 feet. By keeping the unit as close as possible to the building, the lengths of
gravity sewer and wiring will be minimized, keeping installation costs lower while reducing the
chances of infiltration in the gravity flow section.
AC power from the building being served should be used for the grinder pump. Separate power
sources add to installation and O&M costs, decrease overall reliability and frequently represent an
aesthetic issue.
When two dwellings are to be served by a single unit, the station is usually placed in a position
requiring the shortest gravity drains from each home. With multi-family buildings, more than one
grinder pump may be required.
Pipe Selection
The final determination of the type of pipe to be used is the responsibility of the consulting engineer.
In addition, the requirements of local codes, soil, terrain, water and weather conditions that prevail
will guide this decision.
Although pipe fabricated from any approved material may be used, most LPS systems have been
built with PVC and HDPE pipe. Continuous coils of small-diameter, HDPE pipe can be installed with
automatic trenching machines and horizontal drilling machines to sewer areas at lower cost.

<!-- page 240 -->
11
Table 1 compares the water capacity of two types of PVC pipe commonly used: SDR-21 and Sch
40, and one type of HDPE, SDR-11. All three have adequate pressure ratings for low pressure
sewer service.
Although both types of PVC pipes are suitable, the three parameters compared in Table 2 illustrate
why SDR-21 is suggested as a good compromise between capacity, strength, friction loss charac-
teristics and cost.
System Layout
A preliminary sketch of the entire pressure sewer system should be prepared (Figure 3). Pump
models should be selected and their location (elevation) should be noted. The location and direction
of flow of each lateral, zone and main, and the point of discharge should be shown.
The system should be designed to give the shortest runs and the fewest abrupt changes in direc-
tion. “Loops” in the system must be avoided as they lead to unpredictable and uneven distribution of
flow.
Although not shown in Figure 3, the elevation of the shutoff valve of the lowest-lying pump in each
zone should be recorded and used in the final determination of static head loss. Since Environment
One grinder pumps are semi-positive displacement and relatively insensitive to changes in head,
precisely surveyed profiles are unnecessary.
Air/vacuum valves, air release valves and combination air valves serve to prevent the concentration
of air at high points within a system. This is accomplished by exhausting large quantities of air as the
system is filled and also by releasing pockets of air as they accumulate while the system is in
operation and under pressure. Air/vacuum valves and combination air valves also serve to prevent a
potentially destructive vacuum from forming.
Air/vacuum valves should be installed at all system high points and significant changes in grade.
Combination air valves should be installed at those high points where air pockets can form. Air
release valves should be installed at intervals of 2,000 to 2,500 feet on all long horizontal runs that
lack a clearly defined high point.

<!-- page 241 -->

Air relief valves should be installed at the beginning of each downward leg in the system that exhibits
a 30-foot or more drop. Trapped pockets of air in the system not only add static head, but also
increase friction losses by reducing the cross sectional area available for flow. Air will accumulate in
downhill runs preceded by an uphill run.
Long ascending or descending lines require air and vacuum or dual-function valves placed at ap-
proximately 2000-foot intervals. Long horizontal runs require dual function valves placed at approxi-
mately 2000-foot intervals.
Pressure air release valves allow air and/or gas to continuously and automatically released from a
pressurized liquid system. If air or gas pockets collect at the high points in a pumped system, then
those pressurized air pockets can begin to displace usable pipe cross section. As the cross section
of the pipe artificially decreases, the pump sees this situation as increased resistance to its ability to
force the liquid through the pipe.
Air relief valves at high points may be necessary, depending on total system head, flow velocity and
the particular profile. The engineer should consult Environment One in cases where trapped air is
considered a potential problem.
Cleanout and flushing stations should be incorporated into the pipe layout. In general, cleanouts
should be installed at the terminal end of each main, every 1,000 to 1,500 feet on straight runs of
pipe, and whenever two or more mains come together and feed into another main.
Zone Designations
The LPS system illustrated in Figure 3 contains 72 pumps and is divided into 14 individually num-
bered zones. Division into zones facilitates final selection of pipe sizes, which are appropriate in
relation to the requirements that flow velocity in the system is adequate and that both static and
dynamic head losses are within design criteria. Assignment of individual zones follows from the
relationship between the accumulating total number of pumps in a system to the predicted number
that will periodically operate simultaneously (Table 3).
Table 4 was initially developed after careful analysis of more than 58,000 pump events in a 307-day
period during the Albany project (4). It was extended for larger systems by application of probability
theory. The validity of this table has since been confirmed by actual operating experience with
thousands of large and small LPS systems during a 34-year period.
Using Figure 3, the actual exercise of assigning zones is largely mechanical. The single pump
farthest from the discharge point in any main or lateral constitutes a zone. This and downstream
pumps along the main are accumulated until their aggregate number is sufficient to increase the
number of pumps in simultaneous operations by one, i.e., until the predicted maximum flow in-
creases by 11 gpm.
Figure 3 shows that zones 1, 2 and 3 end when the number of pumps connected total 3, 6 and 9,
and the number of pumps in daily simultaneous operation are 2, 3 and 4, respectively.
Any place where two or more sections of main join, or where the outfall is reached, also determines
the end of a zone. This design rule takes precedence over the procedure stated above, as seen in

Review
The accumulated data in Table 4 should finally be reviewed for conformity with the criteria of flow
velocity greater than or equal to 2.0 ft/sec and total design head less than or equal to 185 feet. If the
system pressure exceeds 92 feet, the number of cores operating will remain the same and the flow
from each pump will be reduced from 11 gpm to 9 gpm.
Data should be reviewed to determine whether system improvements could result from construction
modifications. As an example, deeper burial of pipe in one or two critical high-elevation zones might
bring the entire system into compliance with design criteria. Environment One should be consulted
in marginal cases and/or concerning:
• Odor control issues
• Frost protection issues
• Excessive static head conditions
• Excessive total dynamic head conditions
• Unusual applications

1.0 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
The Wendover Pines development is located west of the Heber-Overgaard area in Navajo 
County, Arizona. The development is an approximate 294.5 acre proposed residential 
development including 4 acres of commercial development. There are approximately 450 
proposed residential units planned, ranging in lot size from cabins to 2 acre lots. Wendover Pines 
falls within the area of the Certificate of Convenience and Necessity (CC&N) of High Country 
Pines Water Company (HCPWCo) that will provide water service to the development. There is 
no existing water infrastructure to serve Wendover Pines on site. 
 
Water demand calculations were prepared based on the design requirements of the Arizona 
Department of Environmental Quality’s (ADEQ’s) Engineering Bulletins No. 8 (August, 1978) 
& 10 (May, 1978). The estimated water demands for build-out of Wendover Pines are as 
follows: 
 
 Average Day Demand:                    135.9 gpm  
 Peak Day Demand:                     337.2 gpm  
 
The project’s proposed water distribution system will meet the water requirements per ADEQ 
engineering bulletins relevant to the project. A WaterCAD model was developed to ensure 
Wendover Pines’ water distribution network meets all water requirements. See Appendix A for 
WaterCAD model results of the build-out of Wendover Pines.  
 
Wendover Pines will consist of two pressure zones in order to keep pressures within each zone 
between 45 to 100 psi during Average and Peak Day scenarios. PRVs will be located where 
waterline cross the goal boundary. Additional individual pressure reducing valves at service lines 
may be required where static pressure is greater than 80 psi.

<!-- page 253 -->
Wendover Pines  Coe & Van Loo II, LLC  
Preliminary Water Master Plan  Project No.: 1.14.0334301  
 
2 
N:\14 Coe and Van Loo II LLC\0334301\Enviro\Reports\Wendover.Pines.WMP.docx                       November 8, 2019 
2.0 INTRODUCTION 
2.1 Project Description 
The Wendover Pines development is located west of the Heber-Overgaard populated area in 
Navajo County, Arizona. The development is an approximate 294.5 acre proposed residential 
development including 4 acres of commercial development. There are approximately 450 
proposed residential units planned, ranging in lot size from cabins to 2 acre lots. This preliminary 
water master plan presents the proposed water distribution network necessary to provide water 
services to Wendover Pines.  
 
Wendover Pines falls within the area of the Certificate of Convenience and Necessity (CC&N) of 
High Country Pines Water Company (HCPWCo) [ACC Docket No. W-02448A and ADEQ 
Water System ID# AZ0409329] as such HCPWCo has committed to providing water service for 
the development. There are no existing water services within Wendover Pines. All water 
services, water infrastructure, and treatment will be maintained by the HCPWCo. The HCPWCo 
was created to serve the water needs of the High Country Pines residential subdivision, which 
consists of 320 homes and a total of 6.28 acres of commercial property containing the existing 
Circle K gas station residing on 1.09 acres and 5.19 acres of future commercial property, and 
was expanded to provide water service to the Salvation Army “Camp Ponderosa Ranch”. 
 
2.2 Scope of Work 
The purpose of this preliminary water master plan is to identify existing conditions and 
determine the water infrastructure requirements for the proposed Wendover Pines development. 
It will also determine the anticipated Wendover Pines needs for on-site well, booster pumps, and 
storage tank facilities. The water distribution network will be designed per current guidelines set 
forth by ADEQ’s Engineering Bulletin No. 8 (August, 1978) & 10 (May, 1978). 
 
2.3 Project Location 
Wendover Pines is located in Navajo County, Arizona and is surrounded by the Sitgreaves 
National Forest on the West, North, and East sides, with Arizona State Highway 260 to the 
South. The project is approximately 3 miles northwest of Heber. The project is located in 
Township 12 North, Range 16 East, Section 15 of the Gila and Salt River Base and Meridian, 
Navajo County, Arizona. See Figure 1 (Page 4) for a Vicinity Map and Figure 2 (Page 5) for a 
Location Map.   
 
2.4 Land Use and Population 
Wendover Pines is currently an unde veloped forested property. A summary of the proposed land 
use and dwelling units within Wendover Pines is presented in Ta ble 1. The conceptual Site 
Layout Bubble Plan prepared by RVi is presented in Figure 3 (Pa g e  6 ) .  W a t e r  d e m a n d s  f o r  
Wendover Pines are based on total proposed dwelling units and commercial acreage.

2.5 Topographic Conditions 
Wendover Pines encompasses approximately 294.5 acres of undeveloped forest land. The parcel 
decreases in elevation generally from the southwest to the northeast. The total elevation change 
is approximately 269 feet, dropping from 6,835 feet above mean sea level (MSL) near the 
southwest corner of the development to about 6,566 feet MSL at the northeast corner of the 
development. There are a series of small washes across the site that were determined by the US 
Army Corps of Engineers to be non-jurisdictional under Section 404 of the Clean Water Act.

3.0 WATER NETWORK DESIGN CRITERIA 
The following are design criteria used to prepare this prelimin ary water master plan. Criteria 
complies with ADEQ’s Engineering Bulletins No. 8 (August, 1978) & 10 (May, 1978), and the 
2018 International Fire Code. Any deviation from the listed des ign standards shall be noted in 
this preliminary water master plan. 
 
3.1 Design Criteria 
o Average Day Demand Unit Factors: 
 Low Density Single Family Residential (SFR): 120 gpcd 
 Medium Density SFR: 100 gpcd 
 Commercial: 0.8 gpd/square foot 
o Population Factor = 3.5 capita per Dwelling Unit (DU) 
o Peak Day Demand Peaking Factor 
 PF = 2.5  
 Commercial PF = 2  
o Pressures/Velocity: 
 40 to 100 psi during peak Day / Maximum of 5 fps during peak Day  
 Minimum of 20 psi during maximum fire flow / Maximum of 10 fps during maximum 
fire flow 
o Minimum water line size is 8-inch in diameter. 
o Pressure reducing valves will be placed on waterlines that cross the pressure zone boundary. 
o Fire Flow Requirements: 
 1,000 gpm for SFR for 1 hour 
 1,500 gpm for Commercial for 2 hours  
o Valve Spacing: 
 500’ spacing in commercially zones 
 Space to not isolate more than 2 fire hydrants and/or 30 services at a time  
 800’ spacing for transmission mains 
o Maximum headloss for transmission lines is 8-feet per 1,000-feet of transmission main. 
o Fire Hydrant Spacing: 
 500’ spacing in residential subdivisions 
 300’ in commercial areas 
o Minimum Cover: 
 4’ of cover is required. Cover is measured from subgrade to top  of pipe. If there is no 
pavement then finished grade to top of pipe.

5.0 EXISTING WATER INFRASTRUCTURE 
No existing water infrastructure facilities currently exist in the Wendover Pines parcel with the 
exception of approximately 2,000 LF of 8-inch waterline in the southeast corner of the site that is 
a portion of the 8,200 LF delivery line to the Salvation Army’s “Camp Ponderosa Ranch”.  
 
The existing HCPWCo water line will be relocated in Wendover Pines to maintain service to the 
existing Salvation Army’s “Camp Ponderosa Ranch”.  
5.1 Existing Water Wells 
While researching information for the existing wells within the region, two wells with relevance 
were located. One of the wells is located within the High Country Pines subdivision and is 
owned and operated by HCPWCo. The other well serves Camp Shadow Pines which is a Forest 
Service facility located approximately 2 miles to the west of Wendover Pines along Arizona 
State Highway 260. Both wells are located within the Little Colorado River sub-basin and within 
the Little Colorado River Plateau basin. The wells are approximately 2.2 miles apart. A summary 
of ADWR information can be seen in Table 3. The Camp Shadow Pines well was included to 
determine general water quality of the aquifer.

6.0 PROPOSED WATER INFRASTRUCTURE 
A system of 8-inch waterlines is proposed for the Wendover Pines development. A portion of the 
existing 8-inch waterline connecting the Salvation Army’s “Camp Ponderosa Ranch” to the 
HCPWCo system will be relocated to interior roads within the Wendover Pines development.  
 
The conceptual site layout bubble plan for the proposed Wendover Pines development was used 
to estimate and model water demand. A final water system layout will be prepared during the 
final design stage after lotting for the development is established.  
 
6.1 Water Production Required 
A community water system is considered to have an adequate water supply if the well can 
provide peak day demand. 
 
One (1) 350 gpm well will be constructed for Wendover Pines. It is anticipated the new well, 
tanks, booster pumps, and disinfection facilities will be located at a water production site on the 
north side of SR 260 within Wendover Pines. Based on initial investigations, preliminary sizing 
for the well is for a 6-inch, 350 gpm submersible pump set at an elevation of 850 feet below 
ground surface (bgs). 
 
6.2 Water Storage Required 
Storage for the site is dictated by customer usage and fire flow requirements. With average and 
peak day demands of 135.9 gpm and 337.2 gpm respectively, selection of a well pump with a 
capacity less than the peak day will result in the storage tank(s) draining during a peak day event. 
As demand diminishes, the tanks will recover after the peak day.  
 
Fire flow is based on the 2018 International Fire Code Tables B105.1 (1), B105.1 (2), and 
B105.2. Buildings 0-3,600 square feet require 1,000 gpm for a duration of 1 hour for residential 
development (or 60,000 gallons) and 1,500 gpm for a duration of 2 hours for commercial 
development (or 180,000 gallons).  
 
Residential homes larger than 3,600 square feet require higher volumes of fire flow for 2 or more 
hours depending on size and type of construction, see Appendix A. Automatic sprinklers may be 
installed to reduce the fire flow to 50% of the original value for NFPA 13D sprinkler systems. 
Residential homes 0-3,600 square feet may be reduced to a fire flow of 500 gpm for a half hour 
if a NFPA 13D sprinkler system is installed. 
 
Commercial buildings larger than 3,600 square feet require higher volumes of fire flow for 2 
hours or more. Automatic sprinklers may be installed to reduce the fire flow required to 25% the 
original value but shall not be less than 1,000 or 1,500 gpm for NFPA 13 or 13R sprinkler 
systems respectively.  
 
For this preliminary water storage requirement calculation, it is assumed all buildings are 0-
3,600 square feet.

Water storage is the sum of average day demand at buildout and the largest fire flow 
requirement. To meet the requirement for commercial development fire flow the full build-out 
storage volume of 180,000 gallons will be needed. The required storage is listed in Tables 4. It is 
proposed that 340,000 gallons of storage capacity be constructed for Wendover Pines. 
 
Table 4 – Wendover Pines System Storage Requirements 
 
Line Item Storage Demand Calculation Value Unit 
Average Day Demand: 135.9 gpm x 60 min/hr x 24 hr/day 195,760 gallons 
Fire Flow Demand: 1,500 gpm x 2 hours x 60 min/hour 180,000 gallons

Less: Well Production During 2-hr Fire 350 gpm x 2 hours x 60 min/hr 42,000 gallons 
Total 333,760 gallons 
Proposed Total Storage 340,000 gallons 
 
6.3 Booster Pump Capacity Required 
To serve Wendover Pines, the development will be required to provide adequate booster pump 
capacity equal to the peak day demand for domestic flows in addition to the higher of 1,000 gpm 
for residential fire flows or 1,500 gpm for commercial fire flows. 
 
It is proposed that a pre-engineered, skid mounted booster pump station be provided. Pump 
capacities and operation will be designed to meet average, peak day, and peak day plus fire flow 
demands with the largest pump out of service. Provisions will be made in the booster pump 
station design to cost effectively add pumps to the skid when needed. The required pump 
capacity is listed in Table 5. 
 
Table 5 – Wendover Pines System Booster Pump Requirements 
 
Line Item Booster Pump Demand Value Unit 
Peak Day Demand: 337.2 gpm 
Fire Flow Demand: 1,500 gpm 
Total Booster Pump Capacity Required 1,837 gpm 
 
6.4 Water Treatment Required 
Water treatment may be required for Wendover Pines based on the water quality testing results 
from the proposed wells. Water quality testing will take place when the proposed well is drilled 
and equipped. It is anticipated that water treatment may not be required as existing wells in the 
area appear to have good water quality. Disinfection will be provided in all cases, however. If 
water treatment is required, treatment capacity shall be equal to the peak day demand.

7.0 WATER NETWORK MODELING 
7.1 Network Analysis Domestic Demands 
The proposed Wendover Pines water distribution systems has been modeled to evaluate build-out 
of the system. The model utilizes an on-site water reservoir with pumps to simulate the proposed 
booster pump stations. 
 
The network analysis for the distribution system was completed using WaterCAD version V8i. A 
model was created and modified as necessary to demonstrate that the water infrastructure meets 
the water system design criteria. The network was analyzed for average day, peak day, and peak 
day plus fire flow demand conditions. Demands were allocated to the nodes based on the 
conceptual site layout bubble plan shown in Figure 3 for Wendover Pines. A detailed water 
network layout will be made when a finalized lotting layout is established for Wendover Pines. 
Residential and commercial fire flows were assigned to each appropriate node independently to 
verify available pressure and flow for the system during a fire flow event.    
 
The pipes were sized based on pressure requirements for average day, peak day, and peak day 
plus fire flow, as described in Section 3.0. Input parameters of the water distribution system 
modeling include: 
 
o Pipe Diameters (inches) 
o Elevations of nodes/junctions (feet) 
o System Water Demands (gpm) 
o Residential Fire Flow = 1,000 gpm 
o Commercial Fire Flow = 1,500 gpm 
o Hazen-Williams, C=130 (DIP), C=150 (PVC) 
o Initial Pressure/HGL 
o Zone 
Output parameters include but are not limited to: 
 
o Velocities (fps) 
o Pressure (psig) 
o Head Loss (feet) 
o Flow Rates (gpm) 
 
7.2 Build-out Modeling Results 
The WaterCAD model uses a reservoir to reflect water storage. The detailed results of the 
WaterCAD analysis for the proposed Wendover Pines development is presented in Appendix B 
and summarized in Table 6.

7.3 Pressure Zones 
Two pressure zones have been created in order to keep pressures within Wendover Pines within 
45 to 100 psi during Average and Peak Day scenarios. See the WaterCAD layout in Appendix C 
for locations of the four (4) PRVs within Wendover Pines.  
 
Zone 1 (upper zone) exists on the western side of Wendover Pines and Zone 2 (lower zone) 
exists on the eastern side of Wendover Pines. The Zonal boundary occurs along contour 6,753 
feet MSL. The PRVs are installed along this boundary to reduce pressures from the upper to the 
lower pressure zone. All PRVs are set to deliver a down-gradient pressure of 45 psi.

8.0 SUMMARY 
This preliminary water master plan presents the proposed water distribution system, and an 
overview of the proposed facilities necessary to provide water services to the proposed 
Wendover Pines development at build-out. The following summarizes CVL’s findings: 
 
o Wendover Pines is an approximate 294.5 acre proposed residentia l development including 4 
acres of commercial development. There are approximately 450 pr oposed residential units 
planned, ranging in lot size from cabins to 2 acre lots. 
o There is no existing water infrastructure or facilities constructed to serve the Wendover Pines 
Property. The High Country Pines Development located to the sou th of Wendover Pines is 
served by the HCPWCo water syste m. Wendover Pines will relocate  a portion of the 8-inch 
waterline serving the Salvation Army’s “Camp Ponderosa Ranch” t o within the Wendover 
Pines roadway network. 
o Water demands for Wendover Pines at build-out are: 
 Average Day Demand:                        135.9 gpm 
 Peak Day Demand:                         337.2 gpm  
o Well production capacity required for Wendover Pines at build-o ut is 337.2 gpm. It is 
proposed that one (1) well producing a minimum of 350 gpm be drilled to this requirement.  
o Storage capacity required for Wendover Pines is 340,000 gallons. 
o Booster pump capacity required for Wendover Pines is 337.2 gpm for domestic and 1,500 
gpm for fire flow for a total of 1,837.2 gpm. Pump capacities a nd operation will be designed 
to meet water demands with the largest pump out of service. 
o It is anticipated that water treatment may not be required as e xisting wells in the area appear 
to have good water quality. Disinfection will be provided in al l cases, however. If water 
treatment is required, treatment capacity shall be equal to the peak day demand. 
o The proposed water distribution system for Wendover Pines satis fies all design criteria listed 
in Section 3.0.  
o Individual pressure reducing valves on service lines may be req uired where static pressure is 
greater than 80 psi. 
o Two pressure zones have been created in order to keep pressures  within Wendover Pines 
within 45 to 100 psi during Average and Peak Day scenarios. Zon e 1 (upper zone) exists on 
the western side of Wendover Pines and Zone 2 (lower zone) exis ts on the eastern side of 
Wendover Pines. The Zonal boundary occurs along contour 6,753 f eet MSL. The PRVs are 
installed along this boundary t o reduce pressures from the uppe r to the lower pressure zone. 
All PRVs are set to deliver a down-gradient pressure of 45 psi.

ATTACHMENT B
Heber Overgaard Fire District 
Division of Administration
FROM THE OFFICE OF THE FIRE CHIEF
8/28/2019
RVi Planning + Landscape Architecture 
Attn: Byron Easton 
120 South Ash Avenue 
Tempe, AZ 85281
Subject: "Will Serve" Letter for Fire Services for the Wendover Pines Project 
Dear Mr. Easton:
In response to your request, this letter will serve as confirmation that the Heber-Overgaard Fire District has 
determined that the Wendover Pines Project, located at 320 acres of the North of Section 15, Township 12 
North, Range 16 East, Navajo County Parcel's, 207-04-006A & 207-04-007B resides within the Heber-Overgaard 
Fire District boundaries.
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District will serve the area, providing structural and wildfire suppression, emergency 
medical services, fire prevention activities and other services deemed appropriate for the District.
We look forward to working with you and the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Department to make this 
project a success for the Communities of Heber-Overgaard. Should you have any questions or need more 
information, please call me aV(928) 535-4346.
Chief D. McCluskey 
Office (928) 535-4346 Ext 106 
Cell (928) 240-4149 
Fire Chief / Paramedic 
Heber-Overgaard Fire District
The mission of the Heber-Overgaard Fire District is to serve the people and 
to protect lives and property by providing fire, rescue, 2 nd emergency medical services.
2061 LUMBER VALLEY RD, OVERGAARD, ARIZONA 85933
Rev 7-19

<!-- page 329 -->
NAVAJO N  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & ZONING 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: January 28, 2020 
Case No:  19-97 & 19-98 
Action:   Master Site Plan & Zone Change 
Applicant/Owner: Wendover Pines LLC 
Project Name: Wendover Pines Master Site Plan and Zone Change 
PARCEL INFORMATION  
 Address: No Address 
 
 APN:  207-04-006A & 207-04-007B 
 
 Legal Desc: Township 12 North, Range 16 East, Section 15 of the Gila and Salt River  
  Meridian in the Heber area. 
 
 District: IV 
 
 Area:  Heber, AZ 
 
 Parcel Size: Approximately 72.12 & 236.63 Acres, respectively 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  
 
The applicant has requested approval of a Master Site Plan for up to 450 residential units, a 
commercial-retail space, and open space on APN 207 -04-006A and 207-04-007B.  Additionally, they 
have requested a Zone Change on 207-04-007B from A-General to Special Development, which is 
contingent upon the aforementioned Zone Change. 
 
SURROUNDING USES:  
 
Direction Zoning/Usage 
North  U.S. Forest Service Land 
South SD, Single-Family Residential, Commercial, and Open Space  
East  U.S. Forest Service Land

<!-- page 330 -->
West U.S. Forest Service Land 
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The subject property is located in the Heber area along State Route 260 and Forest Service Road 504. 
 
In the past, APN 207-04-006A has been approved for: 
 
- A 142 residential unit development with 6.35 acres of commercial through Board of 
Supervisors (B.O.S.) Resolution 89-84. 
- A Master Planned Commercial Shopping Complex through the B.O.S. Resolution 02-86. 
- The development of “High Country Apartments” through B.O.S. Resolution 32-87. 
- Four, twenty-four-unit apartments through B.O.S. Resolution 51 -88. 
- A Zone Change to Special Development through B.O.S. Resolution 19-97. 
 
At the Planning and Zoning Commission Hearing, held on December 19th, 2019, the Planning and 
Zoning Commission unanimously recommended approval of the Master Site Plan on APN 207-04-
006A and 207-04-007B and Zone Change from A-General to Special Development on APN 207-04-
007B. 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION: 
 
The proposed project area is in the Heber area along State Route 260 and Forest Service Road 504.  
The total proposed project area is for just under 309 acres, with a total of up to 450 residential units.  
This project is directly surrounded by U.S. Forest Service Land and is across from High Country 
Pines, a similar type of development with Special Development zoning. 
 
This proposal calls for a number of usages and densities.  The proposed usages on this property are as 
follows: 
 
Zoning District Total Units 
WP-7 (Single-Family, 7,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot Size) 260 
WP-10 (Single-Family, 10,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot 
Size) 
 
WP-20 (Single-Family, 20,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot 
Size) 
 
115 
WP-40 (Single-Family, 40,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot 
Size) 
 
53 
WP-80 (Single-Family, 80,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot 
Size) 
 
10 
WP-MF (Multi-Family, 6,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot Size)  
 
12 
WP-CR (Commercial-Residential) 
 
N/A

<!-- page 331 -->
The above zoning districts also have development standards and allowable uses, as required of Special 
Development.  The applicant’s zoning districts closely mirror Navajo County’s single-family, multi-
family, and commercial-residential districts as far as allowed uses.  The only land uses that are 
specifically excluded at this time are adult oriented businesses, medical marijuana dispensaries, 
medical marijuana dispensary offsite cultivation locations, and mobile homes. 
 
The applicant has indicated open space and amenities in order to accommodate for higher levels of 
densities within certain portions of the property.  The developer intends to have pathways along entry 
roads that connect to surface trails throughout the community and open space, with a central 
community park for the development containing amenities (ramada, fire pits, bbq grills, and outdoor 
game activities). 
 
In order to create a buffer with the National Forest, the applicant has indicated there will be a 30-foot 
setback/buffer from the National Forest area where perimeter walls, landscaping and backyards can 
exist, but no structures or walls over 8 -feet in height. 
 
This development will have its own water system designed to service its residents.  The applicant is 
anticipating the need for an on-site well, booster pumps, and storage tank facilities.  The system will 
be designed per Arizona Department of Environmental Quality requirements.  The applicant intends to 
phase the water systems with the phasing of development for this project. 
 
In addition to a self-contained water system, this development will have its own sewer system.  Due to 
the topography of the site, the system will utilize a low-pressure force main with an individual grinder 
pump per home.  It is the intent of the developer to construct the wastewater treatment plant in phases 
as the phasing of Wendover Pines’ also occurs.  The proposed location for the wastewater treatment 
facility is on the eastern side of Forest Service Road 504, toward the northeast of the development. 
 
The developer has either organized or is in the final stages of organizing other utilities and services 
that would be necessary for a development, including: power, fiber, cable, and waste disposal. 
 
If approved, the applicant is hoping that this project will be phased based on market demand and 
purchases with initial phases.  As mentioned above, the applicant will be phasing infrastructural 
improvements to meet the needs of residents. 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
 
ARTICLE 17 – SPECIAL DEVELOPMENT ZONE 
 
Section 1701 – Purpose.  
 
1. The purpose of the Special Development zone is to provide for the development of subdivisio ns 
and other land use projects containing various dwelling types, necessary commercial centers, off 
street parking areas, open space areas for recreation, and to preserve a rural atmosphere. 
 
2. Deviations from design standards for the specific zone or zones may be permitted provided open 
spaces are developed and maintained for the use of the residents and provided further that the 
parcel size, soil conditions, terrain, ground cover and other significant natural features favor this 
type of development.

<!-- page 332 -->
Section 1702 – Provisions Required Within the SD Zone 
 
Planned Unit Development segments are required within the Special Development Zone.  A Planned 
Unit Development or a Special Development project will provide for: 
 
1. Careful application of design which shall be encouraged in order that a more functio nal, 
aesthetically pleasing and harmonious living and working environment may be achieved within 
the County which otherwise may not be possible by strict adherence to the o ther articles of this 
ordinance.  In all cases a Planned Unit Development design shall together with dwelling types 
and land use, complement each other and harmonize with existing and proposed land uses in the 
area.  
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:  
  
The proposed development is contained within the Heber/Overgaard Area Plan, and as such, both this 
Area Plan and Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan must be consulted. 
 
Heber/Overgaard Area Plan 
 
Public Facilities and Services Element 
 
Issue: Utilities 
 
Goal: Provide utilities that are not currently available in order to help keep total utility costs low. 
 
 Objective 2: Develop sewer systems in the residential subdivisions so density of houses does 
 not create problems with septic tanks. 
 
- The developer is intending to create their own sewer system for this residential 
subdivision.  The wastewater treatment facility is indicated on the subject Master 
Development Site Plan.  In addition to explaining and showing plans for a wastewater 
treatment facility, the applicant has submitted a Preliminary Wastewater Master Plan. 
 
 Objective 3: Research alternatives to Arizona Water Co., to lower utility rates for water. 
 
- The is providing their own water system for their development, which will service the 
residents of the community. 
 
  Implementation Strategy 1: Require future developers of 6 or more dwellings per  
  parcel to provide sewer and sewage treatment facilities. 
 
o This proposed development is well above 6 dwellings and, as mentioned above, the 
applicant intends to provide sewer and sewage treatment facilities. 
 
Economic Element 
 
Issue: Tourism 
 
Goal: Benefit from our natural resources through the development of the tourism industry.

<!-- page 333 -->
Objective 1: Establish recreational development. 
 
- This development is establishing recreational development internally through the inclusion 
of walking paths and trails as well as a centrally located park area containing a number of 
amenities. 
 
 Objective 2: Establish recreational facilities. 
 
- See Objective 1 response above. 
 
 Objective 3: Establish recreational paths. 
 
- See Objective 1 response above. 
 
  Implementation Strategy 1: Encourage Navajo County to support and establish  
  recreational development in and around our area. 
 
  Implementation Strategy 2: Encourage private enterprise to support and establish  
  recreational development in and around our area. 
 
o The applicant is looking to support recreational uses through their inclusion 
internally.  This implementation strategy indicates that the Heber-Overgaard Area 
Plan supports this type of development. 
 
Land Use Element 
 
Issue: Residential 
 
Goal: Provide residential areas that are composed of quiet, safe neighborhoods that have easy access 
to schools, retail establishments, employment, and service establishments. 
 
 Objective 1: Minimize denuding of vegetation in new development areas – maintain rural 
 environment. 
 
- The narrative for this project has indicated the importance of the surrounding Ponderosa 
Pine Trees both to the development and the area.  Based on this importance, the developer 
has indicated that the development will be looking to maintain as much of the natural 
environment and vegetation as possible. 
 
Issue: Commercial 
 
Goal: Encourage retail, wholesale, service, and office uses with limited manufacturing capabilities. 
 
 Objective 1: Provide a vegetative buffer zone if a commercial area is to be located adjacent to 
 residential area. 
 
- The applicant will need to provide some form of a vegetative buffer between their 
commercial portion of their proposal and the residential portions of their project.   
 
 Objective 2: Neighborhood shopping centers or group commercial developments require

<!-- page 334 -->
suitable locations with controlled access. 
 
- The commercial-residential portion of this development is located directly adjacent to State 
Route 260.  The location for commercial-residential is also a direct point of access for the 
entire development, making it a suitable, controlled point of access. 
 
 Objective 5: Locate commercial areas for services and retail businesses convenient to 
 residential areas and locate on principal arterial, minor arterial, or collector roadways. 
 
- The commercial proposal with this community is located off of a major roadway, directly 
abuts the residential portions of this development, and is across from the already-
established High Country Pines subdivision. 
 
 Objective 6: Land area to encompass up to 5 acres in commercial developments. 
 
- The total proposal for the commercial portion of this development is 4.0 acres, which is 
under the 5-acre maximum suggestion made by the Area Plan. 
 
Issue: Parks/Greenbelts and Open Space 
 
Goal: Leave open space areas in a natural state for aesthetic and scenic purposes. 
 
 Objective 1: Maintain the rural and pioneer heritage of Heber/Overgaard by keeping essential 
 open space areas. 
 
- This development is maintaining essential open space areas internally and is including a 
30-foot buffer between the development and National Forest land where fencing, 
backyards, and smaller accessory structures can be built or maintained, but where nothing 
above 8 feet in height shall be constructed. 
 
Navajo County Comprehensive Plan 
 
4.0 Land Use Element 
 
4.7 Goals and Policies 
 
Goal 2: To administer the Land Use Element of the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan so as to 
further the purposes of the Plan. 
 
Policy 2-A: Approve zone changes that are consistent with the Land Use Element’s character 
areas, as shown on the Land Use Map.  However, the Land Use Element and Map shall not be 
used as the sole justification for making decisions on zone changes. 
 
- The proposed Zone Change portion of this request is within the Heber-Overgaard Area 
Plan.   
 
Goal 4: To provide equal opportunity for safe, decent, sanitary, and affordable housing for all 
residents of the County, regardless of race, color, religion, mental or physical disability, sex, 
sexual orientation, marital or family status, or national origin, in conformance with the Federal 
Fair Housing Act of 1988 and the Americans with Disabilities Act of 1990 and to provide a

<!-- page 335 -->
range of residential uses that offer housing opportunities for all County residents. 
 
Policy 4-D: Consider developing standards for development density that reflect the needs and 
goals of existing communities, whether incorporated or unincorporated, such as establishing 
medium to high density residential zoning for Community Villages and Rural edge areas and 
low to medium density for developing rural areas. 
 
- Heber-Overgaard is an established community, similar to the Community Village 
Character Area, but larger in size.  As a result, it would make sense that higher density 
developments be created in a hub of the County like this community. 
 
Goal 5: Improve the overall appearance of the County. 
 
Policy 5-A: Maintain natural scenic qualities of the County by identifying and protecting 
cultural resources; protecting wildlife habitat; natural plant communities and riparian areas; 
and encouraging protection of scenic vistas. 
 
- The applicant is currently in the process of working with Arizona Game and Fish 
Department to see if they have any recommendations for protecting plant and wildlife on-
site.  Additionally, the applicant is working to maintain as much of the natural vegetation, 
due to its value to the proposed community. 
 
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development. 
 
 Policy 6-A: Encourage the use of, and provide incentives (such as density bonuses) for, 
 implementation of community master plans. 
 
- The applicant has required some areas of higher density within the development.  To 
accommodate this higher level of density, the plan provides for a wide variety of 
residential uses, as well as open space and a commercial area. 
 
 Policy 6-B: Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses including, 
 but not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping. 
 
- The applicant is providing a buffer along the rear of their property that is adjacent to the 
National Forest.  Directly across the road is another Special Development Subdivision, 
from which this development would not then need to screen itself. 
 
 Policy 6-C: Require paved or other appropriately surfaced access to commercial and industrial 
 uses.  
 
- There is paved access to the commercial portion of this development via State Route 260 
and the developer intends to have paved roadways within the community as well. 
 
Goal 7: Facilitate commercial and industrial development, including business and technology 
uses, where appropriate to increase the sales tax and employment opportunities. 
 
 Policy 7-A: Encourage strategic placement of commercial development so as to attract and 
 locate shopping and employment uses that have paved or other appropriately surfaced access 
 and are easily accessible, particularly in or near established communities and cities, and in

<!-- page 336 -->
communities with air and/or rail access. 
 
- The commercial portion of this proposal is located along State Route 260.  This is a 
strategic location within the properties itself.  Additionally, this location is within the 
community of Heber-Overgaard, making it strategic in relation to this already well-
established community. 
 
Goal 8: Reduce the danger from fire for all residents living in a wildland urban interface  or 
near a National Forest boundary. 
 
 Policy 8-D: Continue to require multiple access routes into new large subdivisions and other 
 major developments for emergency purposes and to improve traffic circulation pursuant to the
 Subdivision Regulations & Requirements. 
 
- The Master Development Site Plan for this proposal shows three separate forms of access 
to this subdivision, which more than meets the requirements laid out. 
 
 Policy 8-E: Continue the review by the Fire Districts in the approval of subdivisions and 
 development projects with their respective jurisdictions. 
 
Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner. 
 
Policy 9-B: Encourage higher density and intensity development in those areas of the County 
where services, including but not limited to water and sewer systems, paved rights-of-way, and 
schools, are available or can be created with new development. 
 
- This development is looking to develop its own water and sewage systems to be self-
sustaining in these respects.  Access leading into this development is paved.  The nearby 
community of Heber-Overgaard already has school systems in place. 
 
5.0 Circulation Element 
 
5.4 Goals and Policies 
 
Goal 1: Provide a range of circulation options that are safe and efficient and that complement 
local communities and the natural environment. 
 
 Policy 1-B: Coordinate land use and circulation planning to encourage comprehensive and 
 efficient development and growth patterns that support adjacent land uses, complement the 
 character of adjacent communities and neighborhoods, and mitigate impacts on the natural 
 environment. 
 
- The proposed use for this case is nearly identical to the residential use to the south, across 
State Route 260.  The applicant is creating buffers with the National Forest Land that 
mitigate impact on this development’s surroundings. 
 
Goal 2: Ensure quality design and development of the circulation system. 
 
 Policy 2-F: Require developers of private circulation infrastructure to meet minimum County 
 standards.

<!-- page 337 -->
- The developer of this project is intending to pave all internal roadways of their subdivision.  
They will be adding turn lanes and there will be improving and widening of State Route 
260. 
 
Goal 4: Provide for safe travel and access to property. 
 
 Policy 4-B: Continue working with developers and property owners to achieve safe and legal 
 access for all properties. 
 
- The proposed community has three points of access and the developer has been working 
with ADOT and the County to make sure that all points of access are both safe and legal in 
nature. 
 
 Policy 4-C: Require developers to install or pay for necessary circulation infrastructure 
 improvements to support access to and within major developments or subdivisions. 
 
- The developer will be providing their own paved roads within the subdivision.   
 
Policy 4-E: Include consideration of, and planning for, pedestrian, equestrian, handicapped-
accessibility and bicycle use when designing circulation infrastructure improvements through 
addition of trails, sidewalks, signage, pavement markings, or other treatments. 
 
- Internally, there will be walking paths and trails that move throughout the development for 
the use of residents. 
 
Policy 4-G: Approve subdivisions or rezonings only where existing street facilities are 
adequate or where the developer agrees to pay for off-site improvements necessitated by the 
proposed development.  Consider adoption of either a development fee program that would 
provide credits for such off-site improvements or another financing mechanism to reimburse a 
developer for a portion of its expenses when later development occurs that benefits from the 
improvements. 
 
- The applicant has been working with ADOT on Traffic Impact Studies and will be 
responsible for any off-site improvements that would be deemed necessary by this study, 
ADOT, and County Engineers. 
 
Goal 5: Improve non-motorized circulation networks and provide more opportunities for 
alternative modes of travel. 
 
 Policy 5-A: Encourage planning for non-motorized circulation infrastructure in new 
 development projects. 
 
- As mentioned above, the applicant is plans to incorporate walking paths and trails 
throughout this development. 
 
Policy 5-C: Encourage major developments and subdivisions to install pedestrian and bicycle 
connections to continue the development of a cohesive system of non-motorized circulation 
infrastructure when such facilities exist on adjacent developments or subdivisions.

<!-- page 338 -->
- See above. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes and 
Article 29. 
 
Postcards for this proposal were sent to all neighbors within 300 feet of the subject properties on the 3 rd 
of December 2019.  A legal ad was run for this item in the Wednesday, December 4th, 2019 edition of The 
Holbrook Tribune notifying of the request.  On the same date, the property was posted with legal notices 
of the Planning and Zoning Commission Hearing. 
 
2. The change is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
The proposed Master Development Site Plan and Zone Change are in compliance both with the  Heber-
Overgaard Area Plan and Navajo County Comprehensive Plan goals, policies, and actions 
(implementation strategies). 
 
3. The change will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare 
of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
As proposed, this development should have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and 
general welfare for nearby properties.

PLANNING AND ZONING COMMISSION RECOMMENDATIONS:  
 
The Planning and Zoning Commission has unanimously recommended that the Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors approve the Master Site Plan on APN 207-04-006A & 207-04-007B, subject to the following 
conditions: 
 
1. All Federal, State, and Navajo County permits required for each lot must be obtained and adhered

<!-- page 339 -->
to. 
 
2. This Master Development Site Plan shall allow for a maximum of 450 residential units. 
 
3. A Traffic Impact Analysis (TIA), Master Drainage Plan, Sanitary Sewer Collection System Design 
Report, and Water Distribution Design Report for the development, prep ared by a Professional 
Engineer, licensed in the State of Arizona, shall be submitted to and approved by the County 
Engineer’s office before the Tentative Plat is submitted to the Planning and Zoning Commission. 
 
4. All required offsite improvements identifie d in the TIA, Master Drainage Plan, Sanitary Sewer 
Collection System Design Report, and Water Distribution Design Report shall be constructed or 
financially assured before the stage of development is constructed that requires the improvement. 
 
5. Sufficient off-road drainage easements shall be made available to accommodate major drainage as 
required. 
 
6. Drainage structures, including detention or retention ponds, shall be proposed with the Final Plat, 
as required. 
 
7. All technical requirements of the Subdivision R egulations shall be met before approval of the 
Tentative Plat.  Variances shall be requested in accordance with Section 4.12. 
 
8. All technical requirements of the Subdivision Regulations and Requirements, Flood Control 
Ordinance, Building Safety Ordinance, a nd applicable codes shall be adhered to and approved at 
the proper Tentative Plat, Final, or Building Permit and construction phase (to include, without 
limitation, the Traffic Impact Analysis, Flood Plain Study, Drainage Study, Construction 
Improvement Plans, and Grading Plans). 
 
9. Approval of the Master Development Site Plan is conditioned upon the submittal of the Tentative Plat 
to the Planning and Zoning Commission, along with proof of compliance with the above conditions, 
within 24 months of the date of Board of Supervisors Resolution.  If this condition is not met, approval 
shall be revoked, and the Master Development Site Plan shall become null and void.  This condition 
shall be conclusively deemed to have been satisfied unless the Board of Supervisors causes a notice of 
revocation to be recorded in the Office of the Navajo County Recorder within 30 days after the 
expiration of the 24-month period (or any extension thereof). 
 
The Planning and Zoning Commission has unanimously recommended that the Nava jo County Board of 
Supervisors approve the Zone Change from A-General to Special Development on APN 207-04-007B.

A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
APPROVING A MASTER DEVELOPMENT SITE PLAN FOR “WENDOVER PINES” 
IN THE HEBER AREA 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Master Development was duly filed on October 3 rd, 
2019 by Wendover Pines, LLC; and  
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 207-04-006A & 207 -04-007B, which are portions of Township 12 North, Range 16 East, 
Section 15 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Heber area; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on December 19, 2019; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony 
and other e vidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that 
the Master Development Site Plan is consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare 
and should be approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby 
approves the Master Development Site Plan, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. All Federal, State, and Navajo County permits required for each lot must be obtained 
and adhered to.

units.

prepared by a Professional Engine er, licensed in the State of Arizona, shall be 
submitted to and approved by the County Engineer’s office before the Tentative Plat is 
submitted to the Planning and Zoning Commission.

Sanitary Sewer Collection System Design Report, and Water Distribution Design 
Report shall be constructed or financially assured before the stage of development is 
constructed that requires the improvement.

drainage as required.

Final Plat, as required.

of the Tentative Plat.  Variances shall be requested in accordance with Section 4.12. 
 
8. All technical requirements of the Subdivision Regulations and Requirements, Flood

to and approved at the proper Tentative Plat, Final, or Building Permit and 
construction phase (to include, without limitation, the Traffic Impact Analysis, Flood

A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING 
A MASTER DEVELOPMENT SITE PLAN FOR “WENDOVER PINES” IN THE 
HEBER AREA  
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Master Development Site Plan was duly filed on 
October 3rd, 2019 by Wendover Pines, LLC; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 207-04-006A & 207 -04-007B, which are portions of Township 12 North, Range 16 East, 
Section 15 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Heber area; and

welfare and should be denied; and

12/19/2019

P & Z Commissioners

1. Chuck Teetsel 
2. Ruth Ann Smith

 STAFF ATTENDANCE
