Meeting held at the Navajo County Board of Supervisors Chambers, Holbrook, Arizona – 
Time:  6:00 PM to 9:06 PM. 
 
Chairman Teetsel called the meeting of the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission to order and explained the meeting procedures to the public.  Mr. Teetsel 
then led the pledge of Allegiance. He then further gave directions to the audience, 
explaining the role of the commission. 
 
ITEM #1 – PLEDGE OF ALLEGIANCE. 
 
ITEM #2 – REVIEW OF AGENDA BY COMMISSIONERS. 
 
ITEM #3 – CALL TO PUBLIC FOR ITEMS NOT ON THE AGENDA. 
 
ITEM #4 – CASE # 19-97 MASTER SITE PLAN, DISTRICT IV: Discussion and possible 
action on a request by Wendover Pines LLC for a Master Site Plan for up to 450 residential 
units, a commercial-retail space, and open space on approximately 295 acres on a portion 
of APN 207-04-006A and 207-04-007B, which are portions of Township 12 North, Range 
16 East, Section 15 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Heber area. 
 
Ms. Phillips presented the item to the Commission, giving the basic details of the

<!-- page 344 -->
area, stating that it was west of Heber and across from the High County Pines 
subdivision. It will also be surrounded by Forest Service land. Sandra gave a brief 
overview of the project, which will cover 309 acres, with 450 units.  There will be 
residential, commercial, and multi -family uses.  She showed the plans for the 
zoning, and the open sp ace. Sandra said that the plans show a mix of zonings 
under the SD zoning, they were planning for lots between 7,000 square feet and 2 
acres in size.  Ms. Phillips pointed out the locations of the water treatment systems 
and their plans for the water line that connects to the Salvation Army Camp, that 
they were planning on relocating the water line to follow the roadway at the cost of 
the developer. The applicant will provide infrastructure and is planning on a sewer 
and water treatment system and have submitted plans for such.  They anticipate 
a low-pressure wastewater system due to topography, they have also submitted a 
traffic impact study and she also showed the developer’s zoning plans for 
comparison with Navajo County’s Zoning Ordinance. 
 
Sandra then recapped the public meeting that was held on 12/11/19 at 6:00 PM at 
the Heber/Overgaard Fire Station, and the questions and concerns that came up 
were: 
 
• Traffic Speed – she noted that staff heard that the residents of High-Country 
Pines were ha ving difficulty with traffic and speeds merging onto State 
Route 260.  The developer and Staff will continue to coordinate with ADOT.  
Turn lanes and acceleration lanes are planned with this development. 
 
• School Impact – the applicant said 450 homes were p lanned, though they 
will be phased . They will start from the east and work their way west in 
phases over a period of approximately 20 years. Staff spoke with the School 
Superintendent.  Given that not everyone owning property will be living 
there year-round, and that many will likely be retirees, they would not have 
any problems with accommodation, and the increase in tax revenue would 
be beneficial.  
 
• Fire Department Concerns – Chief McCluskey said that they were looking 
at the benefits of the additional tax revenue to boost their equipment and 
possibly add employees however are not seeking to put in a satellite office. 
 
• Impact on archeological sites – Archeological studies are not required as 
the project is privately funded on private land.  Protocols require if items are 
discovered to work around the site. 
 
• Dark Skies – Lighting is a priority to the County and the Developer and the 
development will be required to comply with the County’s Lighting 
Ordinance. 
 
• Lack of Availability of Local H ealthcare – Residents voiced concern that 
there were no longer physicians at the local office.  Unfortunately, this is not

<!-- page 345 -->
an issue that is addressed by Planning & Zoning. 
 
• Overflow of Business – Residents were concerned that r estaurants would 
be strained due to increased business from new residents, and that there is 
a shortage of workers. Unfortunately, this is not an issue that is addressed 
by Planning & Zoning.  
 
• “Smoke and Mirrors” was a comment heard from the audience a couple of 
times.  She did research into the history of projects in the area.  It appears 
that Bison Ranch’s density increased over the eight revisions to the Master 
Plan, from 0.6 dwelling units per acre to 1.84. 
 
Sandra noted to the Commission that this initial step is a Zone Change  and a 
Master Plan only.  The details get further vetted in the next steps.   She then read 
the conditions if approved out to the Commission.  Cody also added that the project 
was within the General Plan’s scope and staff has received two letters against and 
one in support. Sandra noted that the applicant was available to speak. 
 
Duane Hunn approached to speak to the Commission, he says that Sand y had 
done such a great job that he almost didn’t need to add anything and would be 
available to answer questions.  Hearing none from the Commission, Chairman 
Teetsel called for people in favor of the project. 
 
Sal Sp errazza came to speak in favor . He sees the project as a bonus to the 
community.  
 
Reverend Paul Nelson asked about the Board of Supervisors meeting and said he 
was neutral to the project and was curious about how the taxes were going to be 
handled, especially when it came to the electric infrastructure.  
 
John Leanos spoke to his concerns about the services provided to the project, as 
he was frustrated with community contractors not being able to handle more 
customers. He was also annoyed that there was incredibly slow internet and was 
going to be further annoyed if he wasn’t going to get upgraded before the new 
project came in.  
 
Steve Dillahunty stated his concern over this being a gated community. Steve was 
also had issues with  quads and other vehicles not being able to go through the 
forest, which was addressed by the Chairman.   Mr. Dillahunty stated, finally, that 
he was concerned about who was planning on caring for the lots. 
 
Phillis Ault said she was under the impression that this would be single-family only 
and felt scandalized by the developer sectioning off an area for multi -family.  She 
was very concerned about a very tall building g oing in. Her biggest concern was 
that they were going to ban access to the area, requiring people to pay a fee to 
enter the commercial and park areas.

<!-- page 346 -->
David Perolis came up to speak to the Commission about traffic lights. 
 
Tom Triolo stated concerns that there would be depreciating value on the property. 
He also voiced complaints on lack of manpower and environmental impact study. 
He also asked about the previous owners and  asked who would maintain the 
roads. 
 
The applicant then was c alled by the Commission to address questions .  
Responses to concerns raised during the meeting were as follows:   
 
1) There would be an HOA;  
 
2) They were waiting to observe the market before they made final decisions on 
gated communities within the project;  
 
3) Common areas would be maintained by the HOA and the roads would be 
brought to County standards so that they could be added into the road 
maintenance system;  
 
4) The multi-family use was desired to create a more neighborly common area that 
was not feasible with single family.  
 
5) The open space was planned to be a private park.   
 
6) As for t he utilities in general, the developers are responsible. Navopache 
Electric is going to be supplying the electricity and the power poles, but there were 
plans to put all lines underground. They have spoken w ith Frontier for amenities, 
but they were also looking at Cable One.  They also wished to implement gas lines, 
however it is not in the area. 
 
Commissioner Howe asked about the ADOT study, and if they had identified what 
they would be necessary.  The applicant stated that they been talking to ADOT 
about this project since spring and will continue to work closely with them for their 
needs; ADOT identified that there will be long turning lanes  needed, so they will 
be widening the road; ADOT hasn’t required them to look into a traffic signal and 
haven’t indicated a need for it.  
 
Commissioner Howe then asked about implementing speed reduction . The 
applicant stated that they have requested a reduction in speed for the area, but 
ADOT was reluctant to grant lower speed limits. They are looking at speeds of 55 
MPH, but are going to be trying to reduce it to 45 MPH.  
 
Commissioner DeCross  asked if t hey had worked with the Fire Chief.  T he 
applicant confirmed that he had spoken with him several times, and his main 
concern was emergencies with the elderly, and did not anticipate that as a huge

<!-- page 347 -->
concern.  
 
Chairman Teetsel called for a motion.  Commissioner DeCross made a motion to 
recommend Approval to the Board.  Commissioner McVicker seconded the motion.  
Motion carried, 6 - 0. 
 
ITEM #5 – CASE # 19-98 ZONE CHANGE, DISTRICT IV: Discussion and possible action 
on a request by Wendover Pines LLC for a Zone Change from existing A-General Zoning 
on an approximately 236.63-acre parcel to Special Development Zoning on APN 207-04-
007B, a portion of Township 12 North, Range 16 East, Sect ion 15 of the Gila and Salt 
River Meridian in the Heber area. 
 
Ms. Phillips just noted some  additional history of the project, and some letters  
received from the public. As this item was tied to the last item and the arguments 
were the same, there was no further business or comments to discuss. 
 
Chairman called for a motion.  Commissioner Smith made a motion to recommend 
Approval to the Board.  Commissioner Howe seconded the motion.  Motion carried, 6 - 
0. 
 
ITEM #6 – CASE # 19-106 SITE PLAN AMENDMENT, DISTRICT IV: Discussion and 
possible action on a request by Brett Ferguson for a Site Plan Amendment for up to 50 
residential units and open space on approximately 10.61 acres on a portion of APN 206-
01-033 and 206-01-034, which are portions of Township 12 North, Range 17 East, Section 
34 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Overgaard area. 
 
Sandra presented the item, noting that it was on the opposite side of 
Heber/Overgaard, opposite Bison Ranch, next to an illegal , wildcat subdivision.  
The applicant’s plan was very similar to what was previously approved, so the item 
before the Commission is a Site Plan Amendment.  The previous applicant  had 
completed a Traffic Impact Study, which indicated there was no need for a turning 
lane into the p roperty from Co lumbia Lane.  The new owner is requesting an 
amendment so that they can  implement a park model cabin concept.  They have 
been working with ADEQ to make sure their alternate septic system meets 
requirements for sewer, and the applicant will have emergency access coming off 
of State Route 260. The setbacks for the property will meet the requirements for 
single-family subdivisions.  Staff is requiring that there be some landscape 
screening along the roads.  Sandra then read out the conditions for approval of the 
project.  It was noted that the applicant is requesting park model cabins, however 
until the lots are purchased, the applicant is asking for seasonal allowance of RVs 
to rent on spaces until a cabin is constructed.  The RV’s will not be allowed on the 
property from October-February.  All engineering and other requirements will be 
standard. Ms. Phillips wanted to point out that there was going to be drainage that 
the applicant will have to let run through the lot during hundred-year-event flooding.  
 
Cody noted that staff had received phone calls and emails of primarily opposition 
to the project, with one letter of support.  H e listed the basic concerns  for the 
Commission.

<!-- page 348 -->
The Chairman then called the applicant to speak. 
 
Applicant, Mr. Brett Ferguson, stated he has lived in the area for a long time and 
said that while he was not a developer, the project was important to him.  He 
wanted to make clear that there would be very manicured landscaping and they 
were renting lots to put very specific park models in. He planned on having three 
models for prospective buyers to choose from.  Buyers would own the park model 
and would rent out the space in the park.   
 
The Commission talked at length with the applicant about his rules and regulations, 
voicing concerns over ho w he was planning on controlling  noise, pets , traffic, 
evictions, groundskeeping, and so forth. 
 
The Chairman then called for public comment in favor, and finding none, called 
for those opposed. 
 
Ken Floberg approached the Commission with the concern that this was just going 
to be another RV park and asked if the splash pad would be open to the public .  
The Commission confirmed with the applicant that it would not be open to the 
public.  
 
Karol Taylor had a petition that objected to the project, which she submitted to staff 
before speaking.  She asked if there was going to be a time limit for how long this 
project would allow for the placement of RVs before they were phased out.  The 
Chairman responded that it was unlikely that such things could be implemented 
or enforced. 
 
Craig Albright noted that the Commissioners had already voiced a lot of his 
concerns and was mostly worried about the ratio of RV’s to park models.  He also 
provided the Commission with some research he had done, noting the last page 
for the impact on the view.  
 
Pete May came forward to speak.  His property borders the project. He noted the 
50 units on ten acres,  with the opinion that density would be an issue.  He further 
stated that this project is a campground and asked the Commission not to approve 
this. 
 
Bill Bowman came up to speak up about how his property value w ould be 
decreased by the project and is concerned about the transients coming in and out 
of the community. 
 
Magnus MacLeod came up to speak about his concern about Toy Haulers .  His 
property monitors a gate that was removed by the airpark, and he notes that there 
are at least a hundred cars that go through there daily.  He pointed out that Tract 
B of the Airpark will be open to the public and ATVs, despite being private property, 
especially with the ability for children to drive them.

<!-- page 349 -->
Sherry Watson and Alan Roberts questioned the Commission about water rights. 
The Chairman replied that those are under the jurisdiction of Arizona Department 
of Water Resources and they have no say in that regard.   
 
David Perolis stated that he had bought property in the area to have a second 
home.  His complaints came from being able to hear music from the nearby pizza 
parlor and feeling noise would become a greater issue with this development. 
 
John Chackerian is a resident of Bison Ranch and was concerned that the project 
would be using facilities that Bison Ranch paid for with their HOA and was also 
concerned that they were going to have an RV park there forever. 
 
Alice Franco stated her concerns that the compatibility with the Airpark is ba d, 
devaluing the property, and that the water drainage is going to be a concern. She 
noted Bison Ranch runs sewer into the old seep and had concerns about open 
sewer in the area.  The Chairman asked if she had contacted Arizona Department 
of Environmental Quality about the creek.  She replied she had not but would do 
so.  Sandra and the applicant spoke to the Commission about sewage. Sandra 
explained that she was aware of the drainage to the seep and was looking into 
this. The applicant also explained that he was proposing an alternative septic 
system that would not pump raw sewage into the ground; They will be  using a 
powerful system that exceeds ADEQ requirements, so they did not foresee any 
issues. 
 
After more discussion, the applicant offered to make the project park models only 
to alleviate concerns from the public over RVs. 
 
Chairman Teetsel called for a motion.  Commissioner Howe made a motion to 
recommend Approval to the Board with the modifications to stipulations 6 that no RV’s 
are allowed, they strike the reference to RV’s on 12. Motion withdrawn to implement 
more changes. 
 
After a brief discussion on phrasing, the Chairman called again for a motion.   
Commissioner Howe made a motion to recommend Approval to the Board with the 
modifications to stipulations 6 that Park-models Only shall be parked in spaces, that No 
park models or other RV shall be parked on the property until the development is 
completed on condition 12.  Commissioner DeCross seconded the motion.  Motion 
carried, 4-2 (Smith, McVicker). 
 
ITEM #7 – CONSIDERATION OF APPROVAL OF MINUTES FROM THE COMMISSION 
HEARING OF 11/21/2019. 
 
Commissioner Rhoades made a motion to approve the minutes.  Commissioner 
DeCross seconded the motion.  Motion passed 6 - 0. 
 
ITEM #8 – COMMISSIONER’S COMMENTS AND/OR DIRECTIONS TO STAFF:

<!-- page 350 -->
ITEM #9 – REPORT FROM STAFF TO THE COMMISSION: 
 
ADJOURN MEETING 
 
With there being no further business to come before the Planning and Zoning 
Commission, the meeting was adjourned at 9:06 a motion was made to adjourn the 
meeting by Commissioner Teetsel. Commissioner DeCross seconded the motion.  
Motion carried.

Comments  I would like this email presented at the December 19, 2019 Planning and Zoning meeting since 
we just found out about the planning for Wendover Pines project that apparently has been in 
the works for a long time.  
My wife and I cannot attend this meeting since we have a previously scheduled doctors 
appointment in Mesa in the afternoon of 12/19/2019 but we want to send our objection to 
this plan to change the zoning for Wendover Pines .  
We live in High Country Pines, located due south of the planned development.  
The meeting we attended at the Heber-Overgaard Fire Department last night we very short on 
specifics and long on generalities and lack of specifics given out by the developers.  
People in attendance that have lived in High Country Pines development spoke out about 
promises made by the developers when they developed and sold property in High Country 
Pines but were never kept.  
The developers also made statements that if cultural artifacts were found/discovered during 
the development process that all development would stop until an impact statement could be 
done. This was not done in High Country Pines since we know of places within the 
development that have Native American cultural significance and development was allowed to 
be done for the last 15-20 years.  
This cannot be allowed to happen again !  
There were also issues brought up about the need for more and better fire service and law 
enforcement response times, possibly another substation and fire house since this 
development is on the extreme western edge of Heber and the fire station is about 4-5 miles 
away and the substation is at least 8 miles away.  
There was also issues of betterment of the schools in the Heber-Overgaard area that would 
also be impacted by this new development.

<!-- page 353 -->
We understand that this project is in the initial phase and this rezoning is the first part of 
bringing this development to fruition but it is like the proverbial camel getting his nose under 
the edge of the tent and in our opinion should be stopped right now.  
We are sorry we cannot attend the P & Z meeting on 12/19 but will be watching and looking 
for the results of the 12/19/2019 meeting.  
 
Thank you,  
 
Allen and Lorraine Asplund

Heber, AZ 85029-0254

Comments  I am a homeowner and full time resident in Overgaa rd, AZ. I am very concerned about the 
proposed subdivision, Wendover Pines in Heber, AZ. The points I am concerned about are fire 
safety, law enforcement and trash. This subdivision has 450 homes proposed. Our Fire 
Department is small. They do a great job but are stretched already. 450 more homes. Who will 
pay for the expansion and/or the building of a new fire house. We have very limited coverage 
from the Navajo County Sheriffs Department. These Deputies would be very stretched also. 
And trash. How much trash will 450 homes and families produce. Our Navajo County 
compactor is very small. These services will be pushed to the excess. Who will pay for these 
additional services needed? The Developers/Investors say that the new homeowners will pay 
for these services through their taxes but at first this will not be enough. I foresee the current 
tax payers in Heber-Overgaard having their taxes raised to cover this project. The 
Developers/Investors should be required to fund these additional services until all of the lots 
have been sold.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. e.        
Meeting Date: 01/28/2020  
Title: Request for a Site Plan Amendment - District IV - Rocky Rim Cabins
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Sandra Phillips, Assistant County Engineer/Flood Control Manager
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Sandra Phillips & Cody Cooper
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 09-2020 
approving/denying request for approval of Site Plan Amendment for Rocky Rim Resort Cabins,
for up to 50 residential units and open space on APN 206-01-033 and 206-01-034 located north of
State Route 260 in Overgaard in Township 12 North, Range 17 East, Section 34.
Background:
This project is located on 10.61 acres of vacant land that is adjacent to Mogollon Airpark to the
north and west, a small 38-cabin rental properties on 10 acres to the east, and State Route 260 and
Bison Ranch to the south. 
In 2007, this site was approved for a Master Site Plan for “Cabin Inns at Overgaard” through
Board of Supervisors Resolution 01-07.  This proposal contained 37 cabin sites with livable areas
of either 1000 square feet or 1300 square feet.  The intent of this development was to be a
time-share, resort-style development.  In addition to residential units, the applicant had proposed a
clubhouse/lobby, conference center, fishing pond, swimming pool, and children’s play area.
 
This project is proposing up to 50 cabins on 10.61 acres with a splash pad amenity, detention, an
alternate septic system, water distribution system and open space.
At the Planning and Zoning Commission Hearing on Thursday, December 19th, 2019, the
Planning and Zoning Commission recommended approval of this Site Plan Amendment to the
Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of 4-2.  With the recommendation for approval, a
motion was made that condition six state that “park models only shall be parked at spaces” and
that the reference to RVs in condition 12 read, “No park models or RV’s shall be placed on-site
until all site improvements are complete and approved by the County.”  These conditions have
been amended as moved and are reflected within this report and resulting resolutions.  There is
significant neighborhood opposition to the proposed project as evidenced by the letters of concern
included in the packet for this item.
See the end of the applicant materials for renderings of the proposed development.

<!-- page 362 -->
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of this Site Plan Amendment for Rocky Rim Cabins.

RESOLUTION ____-20 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
APPROVING A SITE PLAN AMENDMENT FOR “ROCKY RIM RESORT CABINS” IN 
THE OVERGAARD AREA. 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Site Plan Amendment was duly filed on December 5, 
2019 by Brett Ferguson to allow for 50 residential units and open space; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 206 -01-033 & 206 -01-034, which are portions of Township 12 North, Range 17 East, 
Section 34 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Overgaard area; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on December 19, 2019; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after c onsidering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that 
the Site Plan Amendment  is consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and 
should be approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors  hereby 
approves the Site Plan Amendment, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. All Federal, State, and Navajo County Permits required for each space must be obtained 
and adhered to. 
 
2. This Site Plan Amendment shall allow for a maximum of 50 residential units. 
 
3. All lighting on the property shall face inward, be shielded, and be in conformance with 
the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance. 
 
4. All park spaces shall have their own pad and utility hookups. 
 
5. Buffering/Screening. 
 
a. A solid wall or fence, at least six (6) feet in height shall be required along the 
western and northern boundaries of this Site Plan Amendment. 
 
b. A vegetative buffer shall be required on the southern and eastern portions of this

<!-- page 364 -->
property, adjacent to State Route 260 and Columbia Lane. 
 
6. Park models only shall be parked at spaces within this park. 
 
7. A Traffic Impact Analysis (TIA) and Drainage Statement for the development, prepared 
by a Professional Engineer, licensed in the State of Arizona, shall be submitted to and 
approved by the County Engineer’s office before construction on-site begins. 
 
8. All required offsite improvements identified in the TIA and Drainage Statement shall be 
constructed or financially assured before the stage of development is constructed that 
requires the improvement. 
 
9. The applicant shall be required to secure emergency access to State Route 260.  This 
access must meet the requirements of A.D.O.T., the Heber-Overgaard Fire District, and 
Navajo County Public Works. 
 
10. Prior to the issuance of any permits for the subject development, the developer must 
submit a complete set of final engineering plans to the Navajo County Department of 
Public Works for final Engineering and Flood Control review and approval.  These 
engineering plans shall include the necessary supporting documentation such as analysis, 
calculations, etc. 
 
11. Any signs related to the Site Plan Amendment allowed by this permit shall conform to 
the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance. 
 
12. No park models or RV’s shall be placed on-site until all site improvements are complete 
and approved by the County. 
 
13. This permitted Site Plan Amendment shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved 
site plan.  Any expansion or change of the Site Plan shall require an amendment to this 
approval. 
 
PASSED AND ADOPTED  by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of 
_____ yeas and ______ nays on this 28th day of January 2020.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________ 
                                                                           Jason E. Whiting, Chairman of the Board 
ATTEST: 
 
________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

<!-- page 377 -->
Letter of Intent
Applicant: Brett Ferguson October 17, 2019
Project: Rocky Rim RV Resort 
Location: 2036 & 2328 State Route 260 
Current Zoning: Special Development (SD)
Although designed as an RV resort, at full build out this project will include 50 luxury “park model” 
cabins, technically classified as Recreational Vehicles but have the appearance of a small cabin. The 
exterior aesthetics will be tightly controlled to maintain a rustic yet modern mountain retreat feel. Added 
landscaping will enhance the existing natural growth of ponderosa, juniper and pinion pines by adding 
color with aspen and maple trees. Additional pines and native shrubs will be used around each space 
to create a more manicured look and appeal. The frontage along highway 260 will have an open split 
rail fence and additional landscaping to create a buffer between the highway and the resort. The West 
and North sides of the property that border residential homes and lots will have solid cedar type 
fencing.
This project was designed to have a more open, natural layout, with plenty of open space and 
landscape areas throughout the development and will include a beautifully landscaped entry and 
monument, a large open space/park area and an approximately 4,500 square foot splash pad. Along 
with other water elements, the splash pad will have a large center “water tower” style feature which will 
be designed to enhance the overall rustic mountain retreat feel. Being at the edge of town, the water 
tower could serve as a landmark to those entering or leaving the Heber/Overgaard area. In addition, the 
splash pad is intended to attract visitors to the Heber/Overgaard and surrounding areas who are 
looking to spend time at the resort and in turn, spend money in Navajo County.
Similar projects to this in nearby areas have a much higher density and generally have a sterile feel 
with spaces in rows versus clusters and lack any real amenities. Rocky Rim Resort will have about 5 
spaces per acre whereas other RV parks in the immediate and surrounding areas have 12 to 14 spaces 
per acre. The need for an RV park is evident by simply visiting other RV parks in the area. During the 
summer, the existing RV parks are full, causing visitors to travel to other locations, many of which would 
otherwise spend entire summers in the area. In addition to being full, most of the existing RV parks 
have full-time summer residents, so having this in mind from the development stage allows for a nicer 
overall look if and when people create a more permanent space. For example, RVs as a more full-time 
placement in an RV park tend to deteriorate the look and feel of the park over time, especially as they 
get older and accumulate permanent fixtures around them. To avoid this in the planning stage, 
traditional RVs will only be allowed on a short-term basis while the park model cabins, which have a 
more timeless cabin look, will be allowed for more permanent, longer term placement. With this in mind, 
the goal is to keep an upscale look and feel as the RV resort transitions from shorter term RV spaces to 
longer term park model spaces while in both cases catering more to families and kids with the park 
amenities.
The development process would begin as soon as possible following permitting with the goal of being 
open and operational sometime before the end of summer 2020. The infrastructure including water, 
sewer, electrical, roadways and space clearing/leveling would be complete as a whole before the resort 
opens. The landscaping would then be done in phases as both weather and build-out permits with the 
intent of adding additional landscaping each year indefinitely to continue to enhance the resort.
10

<!-- page 378 -->
WRITTEN STATEMENT
WATER:
Water for Rocky Rim RV Park will be serviced by a private on-site well system. The well 
registration number is 55-922937. The ADEQ application (attached) and approval to construct is 
in process and will be completed after approval of the Special Use Permit. ADEQ requires water 
samples which will not be available until after construction starts and the well is operational with 
power.
SANITATION/SEWAGE DISPOSAL:
This project will exceed 3,000 gpd and sanitation is being submitted through ADEQ. The 
currently designed wastewater treatment system will be an alternative type septic system 
designed to reduce nitrogen levels and has sufficient capacity and additional reserve space. 
The system will operate similar to a large septic system with 7 collection tanks ranging in size 
from 1,000 gallons to 3,000 gallons. The affluent from these tanks will then enter a 5,000 gallon 
collection tank and an Orenco (AX100) treatment system before making its way to the leach 
field. The application is currently in process with ADEQ.
11

Instructions
Please complete and sign an Approval to Construct (ATC) Application for Drinking Water Facilities to obtain 
authorization to construct a new public drinking water system, modify an existing facility, or make an alteration that will 
affect the treatment, capacity, water quality, flow, distribution, or operational performance in accordance with Arizona 
Administrative Code (AAC) R18-5-505.

General Application Process

below. Please see the Delegated Agencies for Public Water System document to determine where to 
submit your application (county, City, or ADEQ Regional Office).

Division Drinking Water Engineering Review Unit at WQD_DWSER@azdeq.gov, a week in advance. To 
facilitate scheduling the meeting in a timely manner include ‘Tre Application Meeting” in the subject line. Please 
bring all final application components outlined in the checklist above.

NAVAJO N  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & ZONING 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: January 28, 2020 
Case No:  19-106 
Action:   Site Plan Amendment 
Applicant/Owner: Brett Ferguson 
Project Name: Rocky Rim Resort Cabins Site Plan Amendment 
PARCEL INFORMATION  
 Address: 2036 State Route 260 & 2328 State Route 260 
 
 APN:  206-01-033 & 206-01-034 
 
 Legal Desc: Township 12 North, Range 17 East, Section 34 of the Gila and Salt River  
  Meridian in the Overgaard area. 
 
 District: IV 
 
 Area:  Overgaard, AZ 
 
 Parcel Size: Approximately 2.61 & 8.00 Acres respectively 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  
 
The applicant has requested a Site Plan Amendment to allow for the development up to 50 residential 
units and open space on APN 206-01-033 and 206-01-034. 
 
SURROUNDING USES:  
 
Direction Zoning/Usage 
North  SD, Single-Family Residential 
South SD, Restricted Retail Commercial & Mixed Commercial 
East  SD, Single-Family Residential  
West SD, Single-Family Residential

<!-- page 402 -->
BACKGROUND: 
 
In 2007, this site was approved for a Master Site Plan for “Cabin Inns at Overgaard” through Board of 
Supervisors Resolution 01-07.  This proposal contained 37 cabin sites with livable areas of either 
1000 square feet or 1300 square feet.  The intent of this development was to be a time-share, resort-
style development.  In addition to residential units, the applicant had proposed a clubhouse/lobby, 
conference center, fishing pond, swimming pool, and children’s play area.  
 
At the Planning and Zoning Commission Hearing on Thursday, December 19th, 2019, the Planning and 
Zoning Commission recommended approval of this Site Plan Amendment to the Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors by a vote of 4-2.  With the recommendation for approval, a motion was made that condition 
six state that “park models only shall be parked at spaces” and that the reference to RVs in condition 12 
read, “No park models or RV’s shall be placed on-site until all site improvements are complete and 
approved by the County.”  These conditions have been amended as moved and are reflected within this 
report and resulting resolutions. 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION: 
 
The applicant for this case is looking to propose what they have described as 50 “luxury park model 
cabins.”   
 
Though there is some vegetation on-site the applicant has indicated that they wish to add further 
landscaping for the purpose creating a better look to the property.  Along the frontage with State 
Route 260, the intent is to add an open split rail fence and additional landscaping in order to buffer 
between the resort and the highway. 
 
Along the West and North sides of the property, the applicant has indicated that they will be adding 
solid cedar-type fencing. 
 
The applicant has indicated that RVs could be allowed on a short-term basis while park models would 
be allowed permanently. 
 
If approved, the applicant would like to begin their development process as soon as possible with the 
intent of being opened and operated prior to the end of the summer in 2020. 
 
The proposal will service itself with a private on-site well system.  The applicant is in the process of 
working with Arizona Department of Environmental Quality (ADEQ) to get approval for construction 
of this system. 
 
As this project will exceed 3,000 gallons per day, the applicant must work with ADEQ for sanitation.  
In order to reduce nitrogen levels, the applicant will be operating with an alternative type septic 
system.  The applicant has described the system as follows: 
 
“The system will operate similar to a large septic system with 7 collection tanks ranging in size from 
1,000 gallons to 3,000 gallons.  The affluent from these tanks will then enter a 5,000-gallon collection 
tank and an Orenco (AX100) treatment system before making its way to the leach field. 
 
Staff has received a letter from Navopache Electric Cooperative stating they will serve the subject 
properties.

<!-- page 403 -->
Staff has also received a letter from Heber-Overgaard stating that this project is within their Fire 
District Boundaries and that the District will serve the area, providing structural and wildfire 
suppression, emergency medical services, fire prevention activities, and other appropriate services. 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
 
ARTICLE 17 – SPECIAL DEVELOPMENT ZONE 
 
Section 1701 – Purpose.  
 
1. The purpose of the Special Development zone is to provide for the development of subdivisio ns 
and other land use projects containing various dwelling types, necessary commercial centers, off 
street parking areas, open space areas for recreation, and to preserve a rural atmosphere. 
 
2. Deviations from design standards for the specific zone or zones may be permitted provided open 
spaces are developed and maintained for the use of the residents and provided further that the 
parcel size, soil conditions, terrain, ground cover and other significant natural features favor this 
type of development. 
 
Section 1702 – Provisions Required Within the SD Zone 
 
Planned Unit Development segments are required within the Special Development Zone.  A Pla nned 
Unit Development or a Special Development project will provide for: 
 
1. Careful application of design which shall be encouraged in order that a more functio nal, 
aesthetically pleasing and harmonious living and working environment may be achieved within 
the County which otherwise may not be possible by strict adherence to the other articles of this 
ordinance.  In all cases a Planned Unit Development design shall together with dwelling types 
and land use, complement each other and harmonize with existing and proposed land uses in the 
area.  
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:  
 
The proposed development is contained within the Heber/Overgaard Area Plan, and as such, both this 
Area Plan and Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan must be consulted. 
 
Heber/Overgaard Area Plan 
 
Land Use Element 
 
Issue: Residential 
 
Goal: Provide residential areas that are composed of quiet, safe neighborhoods that have easy access 
to schools, retail establishments, employment, and service establishments. 
 
 Objective 3: Provide each recreational vehicle in RV Parks with its own pad and utility 
 hookups. 
 
- Though the ultimate intent of this project is to provide park models, the adopted Building

<!-- page 404 -->
Code of Navajo County considers park models to be RVs by definition.  As a result of this, 
it will be expected, as laid out in this objective, that every space has its own utilities and 
pad. 
 
 Objective 5: Provide vegetative buffer zone along the perimeter of the manufactured home 
 areas and RV Parks. 
 
- The applicant is providing a vegetative buffer zone along the southern portion of the 
properties between the park and the highway. 
 
Issue: Parks/Greenbelts and Open Space 
 
Goal 2: Leave open space areas in a natural state for aesthetic and scenic purposes. 
 
 Objective 1: Maintain the rural and pioneer heritage of Heber/Overgaard by keeping essential 
 open space areas. 
 
- The applicant will be maintaining an open space within their property for the usage of 
residents of their development. 
 
Navajo County Comprehensive Plan 
 
4.0 Land Use Element 
 
4.7 Goals and Policies 
 
Goal 4: To provide equal opportunity for safe, decent, sanitary, and affordable housing for all 
residents of the County, regardless of race, color, religion, mental or physical disability, sex, 
sexual orientation, marital or family status, or national origin, in conformance with the Federal 
Fair Housing Act of 1988 and the Americans with Disabilities Act of 1990 and to provide a 
range of residential uses that offer housing opportunities for all County residents. 
 
Policy 4-D: Consider developing standards for development density that reflect the needs and 
goals of existing communities, whether incorporated or unincorporated, such as establishing 
medium to high density residential zoning for Community Villages and Rural edge areas and 
low to medium density for developing rural areas. 
 
- This proposal is located in the Heber-Overgaard area.  As this is already a well-established 
community and hub of Navajo County, a development of this density along State Route 
260 is appropriate. 
 
Goal 5: Improve the overall appearance of the County. 
 
Policy 5-A: Maintain natural scenic qualities of the County by identifying and protecting 
cultural resources; protecting wildlife habitat; natural plant communities and riparian areas; 
and encouraging protection of scenic vistas. 
 
- The applicant is looking to maintain as much natural vegetation on the property as possible 
and is looking to go further and add in further landscaping to add to the appeal of their 
property as a development.

<!-- page 405 -->
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development. 
 
 Policy 6-B: Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses including, 
 but not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping. 
 
- The developer has indicated that they will be fencing to separate their property from the 
adjacent Mogollon Air Park and will be adding vegetation and split rail fencing to create a 
buffer between their property and the highway. 
 
Goal 8: Reduce the danger from fire for all residents living in a wildland urban interface  or 
near a National Forest boundary. 
 
 Policy 8-E: Continue the review by the Fire Districts in the approval of subdivisions and 
 development projects with their respective jurisdictions. 
 
- Heber-Overgaard Fire District is involved with the review of this project and has worked 
with both the applicant and County to ensure this project will be suitable to the Fire 
District’s and County’s standards. 
 
Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner. 
 
Policy 9-B: Encourage higher density and intensity development in those areas of the County 
where services, including but not limited to water and sewer systems, paved rights-of-way, and 
schools, are available or can be created with new development. 
 
- The proposed development will be developing its own water system and operating an 
alternative septic-type system to take care of all waste created by residents.  The proposal 
is located directly adjacent to State Route 260 and is within Overgaard, which has nearby 
school and emergency services. 
 
5.0 Circulation Element 
 
5.4 Goals and Policies 
 
Goal 1: Provide a range of circulation options that are safe and efficient and that complement 
local communities and the natural environment. 
 
 Policy 1-B: Coordinate land use and circulation planning to encourage comprehensive and 
 efficient development and growth patterns that support adjacent land uses, complement the 
 character of adjacent communities and neighborhoods, and mitigate impacts on the natural 
 environment. 
 
- This proposed development is located to the west of a cabin rental area and directly across 
from Bison Ranch Resort Suites.  To the north and west of this development are different 
portions of the Mogollon Air Park.  The applicant has indicated, and staff is requiring, that 
the development will be screened from the single-family Mogollon Air Park units.  With 
the received submittal, the applicant has indicated that they are intending to add vegetation 
and landscaping to the property where viable.

<!-- page 406 -->
Goal 2: Ensure quality design and development of the circulation system. 
 
 Policy 2-F: Require developers of private circulation infrastructure to meet minimum County 
 standards. 
 
- Navajo County Engineering will need to decide whether or not they deem it necessary for 
the private circulation infrastructure associated with this property to meet minimum 
County standards. 
 
Goal 4: Provide for safe travel and access to property. 
 
 Policy 4-B: Continue working with developers and property owners to achieve safe and legal 
 access for all properties. 
 
- Current access to the property is shown along Columbia Lane.  After discussions with the 
Heber-Overgaard Fire District, it has become clear that there will need to be some form of 
emergency ingress/egress on the property.  Additionally, the applicant will need to 
complete a Traffic Impact Analysis, if deemed necessary by Navajo County Engineering or 
ADOT, and complete necessary roadway improvements as assessed in this Analysis. 
 
 Policy 4-C: Require developers to install or pay for necessary circulation infrastructure 
 improvements to support access to and within major developments or subdivisions. 
 
- If infrastructure improvements are deemed necessary for this development, the developer 
will be responsible for the costs associated with improvement. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes and 
Article 29. 
 
Postcards for this proposal were sent to all neighbors within 300 feet of the subject properties on the 3 rd of 
December 2019.  A legal ad was run for this item in the Wednesday, December 4 th, 2019 edition of The 
Holbrook Tribune notifying of the request.  On the same date, the property was posted with legal notices 
of the Planning and Zoning Commission Hearing. 
 
2. The change is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
The proposed Site Plan Amendment is in compliance both with the Heber-Overgaard Area Plan and Navajo 
County Comprehensive Plan goals, policies, and actions (implementation strategies). 
 
3. The change will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare 
of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
As proposed, with the conditions contained herein, this development should have minimal adverse effects 
on the public health, safety, and general welfare for nearby properties.

                Initial: B.S.C.  Date:  12/12/2019

          Initial: S.P. Date:  12/12/2019

                                                             Initial: S.P. Date: 12/12/2019

                 Initial: C.C. Date: 12/12/2019
    
PLANNING AND ZONING COMMISSION RECOMMENDATION:  
 
The Planning and Zoning Commission has made the recommendation of approval of this Site Plan 
Amendment, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. All Federal, State, and Navajo County Permits required for each space must be obtained and adhered 
to. 
 
2. This Site Plan Amendment shall allow for a maximum of 50 residential units. 
 
3. All lighting on the property shall face inward, be shielded, and be in conformance with the Navajo 
County Lighting Ordinance. 
 
4. All park spaces shall have their own pad and utility hookups. 
 
5. Buffering/Screening. 
 
a. A solid wall or fence, at least six (6) feet in height shall be required along the western and 
northern boundaries of this Site Plan Amendment. 
 
b. A vegetative buffer shall be required on the southern and eastern portions of this property, 
adjacent to State Route 260 and Columbia Lane. 
 
6. Park models only shall be parked at spaces within this park. 
 
7. A Traffic Impact Analysis (TIA) and Drainage Statement for the development, prepared by a 
Professional Engineer, licensed in the State of Arizona, shall be submitted to and approved by the 
County Engineer’s office before construction on-site begins. 
 
8. All required offsite improvements identified in the TIA and Drainage Statement shall be constructed 
or financially assured before the stage of development is constructed that requires the improvement. 
 
9. The applicant shall be required to secure emergency access to State Route 260.  This access must 
meet the requirements of A.D.O.T., the Heber-Overgaard Fire District, and Navajo County Public 
Works.

<!-- page 408 -->
10. Prior to the issuance of any permits for the subject development, the developer must submit a 
complete set of final engineering plans to the Navajo County Department of Public Works for final 
Engineering and Flood Control review and approval.  These engineering plans shall include the 
necessary supporting documentation such as analysis, calculations, etc. 
 
11. Any signs related to the Site Plan Amendment allowed by this permit shall conform to the Navajo 
County Zoning Ordinance. 
 
12. No park models or RV’s shall be placed on-site until all site improvements are complete and 
approved by the County. 
 
13. This permitted Site Plan Amendment shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan.  
Any expansion or change of the Site Plan shall require an amendment to this approval.

Meeting held at the Navajo County Board of Supervisors Chambers, Holbrook, Arizona – 
Time:  6:00 PM to 9:06 PM. 
 
Chairman Teetsel called the meeting of the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission to order and explained the meeting procedures to the public.  Mr. Teetsel 
then led the pledge of Allegiance. He then further gave directions to the audience, 
explaining the role of the commission. 
 
ITEM #1 – PLEDGE OF ALLEGIANCE. 
 
ITEM #2 – REVIEW OF AGENDA BY COMMISSIONERS.

ITEM #4 – CASE # 19-97 MASTER SITE PLAN, DISTRICT IV: Discussion and possible 
action on a request by Wendover Pines LLC for a Master Site Plan for up to 450 residential 
units, a commercial-retail space, and open space on approximately 295 acres on a portion 
of APN 207-04-006A and 207-04-007B, which are portions of Township 12 North, Range 
16 East, Section 15 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Heber area. 
 
Ms. Phillips presented the item to the Commission, giving the basic details of the

<!-- page 410 -->
area, stating that it was west of Heber and across from the High County Pines 
subdivision. It will also be surrounded by Forest Service land. Sandra gave a brief 
overview of the project, which will cover 309 acres, with 450 units.  There will be 
residential, commercial, and multi -family uses.  She showed the plans for the 
zoning, and the open sp ace. Sandra said that the plans show a mix of zonings 
under the SD zoning, they were planning for lots between 7,000 square feet and 2 
acres in size.  Ms. Phillips pointed out the locations of the water treatment systems 
and their plans for the water line that connects to the Salvation Army Camp, that 
they were planning on relocating the water line to follow the roadway at the cost of 
the developer. The applicant will provide infrastructure and is planning on a sewer 
and water treatment system and have submitted plans for such.  They anticipate 
a low-pressure wastewater system due to topography, they have also submitted a 
traffic impact study and she also showed the developer’s zoning plans for 
comparison with Navajo County’s Zoning Ordinance. 
 
Sandra then recapped the public meeting that was held on 12/11/19 at 6:00 PM at 
the Heber/Overgaard Fire Station, and the questions and concerns that came up 
were: 
 
• Traffic Speed – she noted that staff heard that the residents of High-Country 
Pines were ha ving difficulty with traffic and speeds merging onto State 
Route 260.  The developer and Staff will continue to coordinate with ADOT.  
Turn lanes and acceleration lanes are planned with this development. 
 
• School Impact – the applicant said 450 homes were p lanned, though they 
will be phased . They will start from the east and work their way west in 
phases over a period of approximately 20 years. Staff spoke with the School 
Superintendent.  Given that not everyone owning property will be living 
there year-round, and that many will likely be retirees, they would not have 
any problems with accommodation, and the increase in tax revenue would 
be beneficial.  
 
• Fire Department Concerns – Chief McCluskey said that they were looking 
at the benefits of the additional tax revenue to boost their equipment and 
possibly add employees however are not seeking to put in a satellite office. 
 
• Impact on archeological sites – Archeological studies are not required as 
the project is privately funded on private land.  Protocols require if items are 
discovered to work around the site. 
 
• Dark Skies – Lighting is a priority to the County and the Developer and the 
development will be required to comply with the County’s Lighting 
Ordinance. 
 
• Lack of Availability of Local H ealthcare – Residents voiced concern that 
there were no longer physicians at the local office.  Unfortunately, this is not

<!-- page 411 -->
an issue that is addressed by Planning & Zoning. 
 
• Overflow of Business – Residents were concerned that r estaurants would 
be strained due to increased business from new residents, and that there is 
a shortage of workers. Unfortunately, this is not an issue that is addressed 
by Planning & Zoning.  
 
• “Smoke and Mirrors” was a comment heard from the audience a couple of 
times.  She did research into the history of projects in the area.  It appears 
that Bison Ranch’s density increased over the eight revisions to the Master 
Plan, from 0.6 dwelling units per acre to 1.84. 
 
Sandra noted to the Commission that this initial step is a Zone Change  and a 
Master Plan only.  The details get further vetted in the next steps.   She then read 
the conditions if approved out to the Commission.  Cody also added that the project 
was within the General Plan’s scope and staff has received two letters against and 
one in support. Sandra noted that the applicant was available to speak. 
 
Duane Hunn approached to speak to the Commission, he says that Sand y had 
done such a great job that he almost didn’t need to add anything and would be 
available to answer questions.  Hearing none from the Commission, Chairman 
Teetsel called for people in favor of the project. 
 
Sal Sp errazza came to speak in favor . He sees the project as a bonus to the 
community.  
 
Reverend Paul Nelson asked about the Board of Supervisors meeting and said he 
was neutral to the project and was curious about how the taxes were going to be 
handled, especially when it came to the electric infrastructure.  
 
John Leanos spoke to his concerns about the services provided to the project, as 
he was frustrated with community contractors not being able to handle more 
customers. He was also annoyed that there was incredibly slow internet and was 
going to be further annoyed if he wasn’t going to get upgraded before the new 
project came in.  
 
Steve Dillahunty stated his concern over this being a gated community. Steve was 
also had issues with  quads and other vehicles not being able to go through the 
forest, which was addressed by the Chairman.   Mr. Dillahunty stated, finally, that 
he was concerned about who was planning on caring for the lots. 
 
Phillis Ault said she was under the impression that this would be single-family only 
and felt scandalized by the developer sectioning off an area for multi -family.  She 
was very concerned about a very tall building g oing in. Her biggest concern was 
that they were going to ban access to the area, requiring people to pay a fee to 
enter the commercial and park areas.

<!-- page 412 -->
David Perolis came up to speak to the Commission about traffic lights. 
 
Tom Triolo stated concerns that there would be depreciating value on the property. 
He also voiced complaints on lack of manpower and environmental impact study. 
He also asked about the previous owners and  asked who would maintain the 
roads. 
 
The applicant then was c alled by the Commission to address questions .  
Responses to concerns raised during the meeting were as follows:   
 
1) There would be an HOA;  
 
2) They were waiting to observe the market before they made final decisions on 
gated communities within the project;  
 
3) Common areas would be maintained by the HOA and the roads would be 
brought to County standards so that they could be added into the road 
maintenance system;  
 
4) The multi-family use was desired to create a more neighborly common area that 
was not feasible with single family.  
 
5) The open space was planned to be a private park.   
 
6) As for t he utilities in general, the developers are responsible. Navopache 
Electric is going to be supplying the electricity and the power poles, but there were 
plans to put all lines underground. They have spoken w ith Frontier for amenities, 
but they were also looking at Cable One.  They also wished to implement gas lines, 
however it is not in the area. 
 
Commissioner Howe asked about the ADOT study, and if they had identified what 
they would be necessary.  The applicant stated that they been talking to ADOT 
about this project since spring and will continue to work closely with them for their 
needs; ADOT identified that there will be long turning lanes  needed, so they will 
be widening the road; ADOT hasn’t required them to look into a traffic signal and 
haven’t indicated a need for it.  
 
Commissioner Howe then asked about implementing speed reduction . The 
applicant stated that they have requested a reduction in speed for the area, but 
ADOT was reluctant to grant lower speed limits. They are looking at speeds of 55 
MPH, but are going to be trying to reduce it to 45 MPH.  
 
Commissioner DeCross  asked if t hey had worked with the Fire Chief.  T he 
applicant confirmed that he had spoken with him several times, and his main 
concern was emergencies with the elderly, and did not anticipate that as a huge

<!-- page 413 -->
concern.  
 
Chairman Teetsel called for a motion.  Commissioner DeCross made a motion to 
recommend Approval to the Board.  Commissioner McVicker seconded the motion.  
Motion carried, 6 - 0.

ITEM #6 – CASE # 19-106 SITE PLAN AMENDMENT, DISTRICT IV: Discussion and 
possible action on a request by Brett Ferguson for a Site Plan Amendment for up to 50 
residential units and open space on approximately 10.61 acres on a portion of APN 206-
01-033 and 206-01-034, which are portions of Township 12 North, Range 17 East, Section 
34 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Overgaard area. 
 
Sandra presented the item, noting that it was on the opposite side of 
Heber/Overgaard, opposite Bison Ranch, next to an illegal , wildcat subdivision.  
The applicant’s plan was very similar to what was previously approved, so the item 
before the Commission is a Site Plan Amendment.  The previous applicant  had 
completed a Traffic Impact Study, which indicated there was no need for a turning 
lane into the p roperty from Co lumbia Lane.  The new owner is requesting an 
amendment so that they can  implement a park model cabin concept.  They have 
been working with ADEQ to make sure their alternate septic system meets 
requirements for sewer, and the applicant will have emergency access coming off 
of State Route 260. The setbacks for the property will meet the requirements for 
single-family subdivisions.  Staff is requiring that there be some landscape 
screening along the roads.  Sandra then read out the conditions for approval of the 
project.  It was noted that the applicant is requesting park model cabins, however 
until the lots are purchased, the applicant is asking for seasonal allowance of RVs 
to rent on spaces until a cabin is constructed.  The RV’s will not be allowed on the 
property from October-February.  All engineering and other requirements will be 
standard. Ms. Phillips wanted to point out that there was going to be drainage that 
the applicant will have to let run through the lot during hundred-year-event flooding.  
 
Cody noted that staff had received phone calls and emails of primarily opposition 
to the project, with one letter of support.  H e listed the basic concerns  for the 
Commission.

<!-- page 414 -->
The Chairman then called the applicant to speak. 
 
Applicant, Mr. Brett Ferguson, stated he has lived in the area for a long time and 
said that while he was not a developer, the project was important to him.  He 
wanted to make clear that there would be very manicured landscaping and they 
were renting lots to put very specific park models in. He planned on having three 
models for prospective buyers to choose from.  Buyers would own the park model 
and would rent out the space in the park.   
 
The Commission talked at length with the applicant about his rules and regulations, 
voicing concerns over ho w he was planning on controlling  noise, pets , traffic, 
evictions, groundskeeping, and so forth. 
 
The Chairman then called for public comment in favor, and finding none, called 
for those opposed. 
 
Ken Floberg approached the Commission with the concern that this was just going 
to be another RV park and asked if the splash pad would be open to the public .  
The Commission confirmed with the applicant that it would not be open to the 
public.  
 
Karol Taylor had a petition that objected to the project, which she submitted to staff 
before speaking.  She asked if there was going to be a time limit for how long this 
project would allow for the placement of RVs before they were phased out.  The 
Chairman responded that it was unlikely that such things could be implemented 
or enforced. 
 
Craig Albright noted that the Commissioners had already voiced a lot of his 
concerns and was mostly worried about the ratio of RV’s to park models.  He also 
provided the Commission with some research he had done, noting the last page 
for the impact on the view.  
 
Pete May came forward to speak.  His property borders the project. He noted the 
50 units on ten acres,  with the opinion that density would be an issue.  He further 
stated that this project is a campground and asked the Commission not to approve 
this. 
 
Bill Bowman came up to speak up about how his property value w ould be 
decreased by the project and is concerned about the transients coming in and out 
of the community. 
 
Magnus MacLeod came up to speak about his concern about Toy Haulers .  His 
property monitors a gate that was removed by the airpark, and he notes that there 
are at least a hundred cars that go through there daily.  He pointed out that Tract 
B of the Airpark will be open to the public and ATVs, despite being private property, 
especially with the ability for children to drive them.

<!-- page 415 -->
Sherry Watson and Alan Roberts questioned the Commission about water rights. 
The Chairman replied that those are under the jurisdiction of Arizona Department 
of Water Resources and they have no say in that regard.   
 
David Perolis stated that he had bought property in the area to have a second 
home.  His complaints came from being able to hear music from the nearby pizza 
parlor and feeling noise would become a greater issue with this development. 
 
John Chackerian is a resident of Bison Ranch and was concerned that the project 
would be using facilities that Bison Ranch paid for with their HOA and was also

Alice Franco stated her concerns that the compatibility with the Airpark is ba d, 
devaluing the property, and that the water drainage is going to be a concern. She 
noted Bison Ranch runs sewer into the old seep and had concerns about open 
sewer in the area.  The Chairman asked if she had contacted Arizona Department 
of Environmental Quality about the creek.  She replied she had not but would do 
so.  Sandra and the applicant spoke to the Commission about sewage. Sandra 
explained that she was aware of the drainage to the seep and was looking into 
this. The applicant also explained that he was proposing an alternative septic 
system that would not pump raw sewage into the ground; They will be  using a 
powerful system that exceeds ADEQ requirements, so they did not foresee any 
issues. 
 
After more discussion, the applicant offered to make the project park models only 
to alleviate concerns from the public over RVs. 
 
Chairman Teetsel called for a motion.  Commissioner Howe made a motion to 
recommend Approval to the Board with the modifications to stipulations 6 that no RV’s 
are allowed, they strike the reference to RV’s on 12. Motion withdrawn to implement

After a brief discussion on phrasing, the Chairman called again for a motion.   
Commissioner Howe made a motion to recommend Approval to the Board with the 
modifications to stipulations 6 that Park-models Only shall be parked in spaces, that No 
park models or other RV shall be parked on the property until the development is

December 9, 2019
Cody Cooper
Planning & Zoning Division
PO Box 668 
Holbrook, AZ  86025
Dear Mr. Cooper;
Thank you for taking the time to speak with me.
I am extremely opposed to the site plan amendment for up to 50 residential units on APN 206­01­033 and 206­01­034; 
Township 12 North, Range 17 East, Section 34 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Overgaard area.  Overgaard is a 
unique and historical location.  Heber being established in 1883 and the Overgaard Post Office in 1938.
I recently inherited the Mogollon Airpark property Parcel # 206­29­027 from my father. My father chose the Overgaard 
area because of the tranquility of its Forest environment of open woods, unique wildlife, scenic beauty and of course as 
an airpark.  Heber/Overgaard promotes tourism with its relaxing,scenic views and the beauty of the Apache­Sitgreaves 
National Forest.
My opposing concerns are as follows:
1. It is an RV Park for “temporary” visitors. Temporary or non­permanent residents creating them to not value 
Overgaard nor its environment.  High probability of accidents on quads, trespassing, etc.
2. Environmental and wildlife impact.  Many elk, deer and other indigenous wildlife are often seen roaming freely 
within the airpark. Many rules and regulations of the airpark, already preserve these concerns.
3. Limited access to the airpark even though it is close in proximity to walk, ride ATVs, etc.
4. That they will be directly under the normal air traffic pattern of an active private airpark.  There have been 
incidences where new developments have caused the initial homesteads to relocate due to complaints of noise, 
planes, etc.  Mogollon Airpark is an active private airpark and during the Rodeo­Chediski fire, the fire crews were 
given permission from the airpark to utilize the runway for fighting that devasting fire.
5. Impact on current property values?
6. This RV Park would be directly in competition with the Bison Ranch rentals; amongst the other RV parks already 
in existence around Heber/Overgaard.  Compare the occupancy rates of those to see if this RV Park would be 
beneficial.
7. Fire hazards and restrictions adhered to within and around this RV Park site.  Very close to aviation.
8. How would this RV Park impact the light and noise ordinances already in place?
9. How would this RV Park make an impact on traffic congestion?
10. How would this RV Park affect electricity, water and sewer facilities to existing homes and businesses and also in 
relation to our property taxes?
11. Increase in vandalism & property damages.  Causing property insurance increases.
12. Water treatment plant?– impacts environmental, noise, leakage, smell if open air, etc. 
Please review and consider my concerns in your decision making for this amendment.
Thank you for your time and consideration.
Sincerely,
Christine Woolridge

Overgaard address:  2282 Spitfire Dr; Overgaard, AZ  85933

<!-- page 444 -->
TO:                Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
  SUBJECT:    Case #19-106 Site Plan Amendment, District IV 
 
 
Board of Supervisors, 
 
     BACKGROUND.   My wife and I own the residence at 2749 Challenger 
Drive, Overgaard.  Our residence will directly border this proposed development.  We attended the 
Pubic Works hearing in Holbrook 
recently.   All speakers voiced concern and opposition against this 
particular development proposal.  After public comment was cut off, a member of the Navajo County 
Public Works Department  (who had apparently been working with this developer) was allowed to 
present an unopposed pro development pitch for this developer.  The public was not permitted to 
respond.  The project with two minor amendments was passed and moved on to the County 
Commissioners for passage. 
 
FIRST:   This parcel of land this development is slated for was 
originally platted as sub-units IVa and IVb of Mogollon Airpark. The intended use was for smaller single 
lot residential homes compatible with existing residences.  This developer intends to put 50 concrete 
pads on three loops.  Each loop would be centered around a depression which is intended to hold 
intermittent surface water flooding.  A splash park and sewage leach field will be located along Columbia 
Drive at the east end of the development. On this east end, where the splash park and leach field are 
intended to be located, the developer has sunk a well and intends to also locate his development's main 
access route onto Columbia Drive.  This area is a drainage zone for winter snow melt and periodic 
deluges, it experiences occasional flooding and standing water.  Though the developers treatment plant 
may control nitrate levels under normal conditions.  It is doubtful it will function adequately with 
saturated soils.  Runoff and surface water will certainly contain fecal contamination (e Coli).  The 
developer did not present an occupancy cap for this project.  He did however state that his mobile 
cabins (classed as RV's by the state of Arizona) will have a bedroom, loft and fold out beds which could 
accommodate 8 individuals.  So this sewage plant could conceivably be tasked with handling wastes 
from 400 individuals.  Can this treatment plant and leach field seriously deal with this volume of waste in 
these shallow mountain soils? 
Additionally, it was pointed out at the hearing that the Cabin Fever's septic disposal system is in 
violation of it's Navajo County permit.  If the County did not followup and enforce regulations for this 
existing permit will it for this development. 
 
SECOND:  During the recent hearing this developer backtracked on many of his statements.  He 
appeared not to have thought through numerous aspects of management of this development.  He 
apparently will have no host or manager on site to deal with daily problems. No one to deal with quiet 
hours, excess noise, trash, residents who trespass or cause problems for local residents.  Even if an 
individual was assigned management duties.  What type of response time and responsiveness could 
local residents expect from this business enterprise.  (Example:  RV park residents running up and down 
local roads and across resident properties with ATVs).  How will this developer with no track history in 
such an enterprise stop pad renters from short term subleasing of these RV cabins to individuals who 
wish to use them as party cabins?  It was only at the very last minute and prior to the hearing's vote that 
this developer changed his proposal. He went from the daily renting of his development's concrete pads 
to RVs to restricting their use to RV cabins he would sell.  Should we expect him to not rent these cabins

<!-- page 445 -->
out himself, especially if they do not sell.  Would not such a move turn his proposal back into a short 
term RV park? 
 
CONCLUSION:  In short, this developer has many issues he needs to answer before this development 
should seriously be considered. This business plan could work if relocated on a larger parcel of land 
outside of village limits.  However, at the proposed location it is most likely destined to failure and will 
leave an eyesore for the community. 
 
 
Thank You, 
 
Peter and Debra May

RESOLUTION ____-20 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING 
A SITE PLAN AMENDMENT FOR “ROCKY RIM RESORT CABINS” IN THE 
OVERGAARD AREA  
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Site Plan Amendment was duly filed on December 5, 
2019 by Brett Ferguson to allow for 50 residential units and open space; and  
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 206 -01-033 & 206 -01-034, which are portions of Township 12 North, Range 17 East, 
Section 34 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Overgaard area; and 
 
WHEREAS, a Public Hearing, as required by Section 2902(2) of the Navajo County 
Zoning Ordinance, was duly noticed and held on December 19, 2019; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after c onsidering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that 
the Site Plan Amendment is not consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and 
should be denied; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby 
deny the Site Plan Amendment.

<!-- page 449 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 8. a.        
Meeting Date: 01/28/2020  
Title: White Mountain Lakes Sanitary District Dissolution
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Melissa Buckley/Jason Moore
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 10-2020, dissolving the White Mountain
Lakes Sanitary District. 
Background:
The White Mountain Lakes Sanitary District was created by the Board of Supervisors on
December 11, 1984, by Resolution 195-84. This Sanitation District never became operational and
the needs were handled by a private sanitation company.
A.R.S. §48-959: Any district organized under this article shall be dissolved by its board of
directors when all bonds and other obligations of the district are paid or have become barred by
the statute of limitations, and the operation and maintenance functions of the district or the major
part thereof have been taken over by an incorporated city or town, by the county or by a utility.
The conditions of A.R.S. §48-959 have been met.
Recommendation: 
Recommend adoption of the Resolution dissolving this district.

RESOLUTION NUMBER ___-2020 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE BOARD OF DIRECTORS OF THE WHITE MOUNTAIN 
LAKES SANITARY DISTRICT DISSOLVING THE WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES 
SANITARY DISTRICT 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors  created the White Mountain Lakes  
Sanitary District by Resolution 195-84 on or about December 11th, 1984, and;   
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors wa s established as the Board of Directors 
of said Sanitary District, and ; 
 
WHEREAS, the Sanitary District in question never became operational, and the sanitation needs 
of the area in question were ultimately handled by the formation of a private sanitation company; 
and;  
 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 48 -959 allows for the dissolution of an improvement distr ict when all 
bonds or obligations of the District have been paid or are barred by the statute of limitations, and 
the functions of the District have been taken over by an incor porated city or town, by the county 
or by a utility, and;   
 
WHEREAS, the conditions set forth in A.R.S. § 48-959 have been met. 
 
THEREFORE, IT IS HEREBY RESOLVED by the Board of as follows: 
 
1.)  The White Mountain Lakes Sanitary District is hereby dissolved. 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED at Holbrook, Arizona, on _______________, 2020. 
 
BOARD OF DIRECTORS OF THE WHITE
